Zuko’s nostrils flared with indignation, but he did as he was told, every movement of his body radiating defiance. Zhao reached out to grip his chin lightly and the boy startled, attempting to draw back. Zhao tightened his hold. “Are you seriously making demands of me?”

“I’m *asking,*” Zuko retorted, in a tone that was definitely much closer to a demand than a request. “If you want him to live long enough to be useful, then you need to give me the means to keep him alive.”

“*Need* to?” Zhao moved quickly, striking through the bars and gripping Zuko by the back of the neck like he was scruffing a kitten, giving him a light shake, then releasing him in the same motion. He trailed his fingers along the boy's jaw as he withdrew his hand. Zuko shuddered, his expression completely bewildered. It put Taiju in mind of the fleeting touches you would use to get a feral animal used to human contact.

It was disquieting to watch and made a bad feeling pool in Taiju’s gut. He desperately wanted some guidance on what he should do in this situation, should he take some action to prevent whatever unpleasantness Zhao was planning? Or would that go against his current instructions? Zuko’s death had been ordered by the Fire Lord and it had to happen, that much was clear. It would be better, *cleaner,* if he met with an accident, but getting caught arranging such a thing would put everything Taiju had achieved at risk.

He could write and ask, but what if the reply was not what he wanted to read? Worse, what if the reply put him at odds with the Fire Lord’s sentence of death? He didn’t think it would, but it *could,* and then what would he do? Loyalty was a frightening thing and it sent you in strange directions sometimes.

“Yes!” Zuko’s disgruntled yell broke Taiju from his increasingly spiralling thoughts. “*Need* too. If you want him to live. If you don’t, then don’t bother.” His voice held none of the confusion still on his face, and his posture was still one hundred percent arrogant defiance.

Zhao looked at him with an expression of condescension, like he was looking at a small child throwing a tantrum. “I told you, Zuko, I will provide medical supplies. You may treat his wounds, I want him healthy by tomorrow.”

“Tomorrow is too soon,” Zuko shot back, “He needs rest and time for his injuries to recover, there’s no point hurting him again. He’ll just die on you.”

“Will he? I wonder. Perhaps between you, you can come up with some information that will be useful enough we won’t need to repeat today's fun and games and we can give the little water whelp a break, hmm? Think it over.” Zhao turned to look at Taiju, making his spine straighten automatically. “Second Lieutenant, please fetch the items his ex-royal highness has requested.”

Taiju saluted and headed for the door, relieved to finally escape.

He wished he could leave his doubts behind as easily.

…………….

Sokka had never been in so much pain, constant crashing waves of it. He was vaguely aware he was crying and his nose was running disgustingly, but he couldn't move his arms up to wipe it off. All he could do was remain crouched on the floor like some sort of deranged badger frog, wrapped in his own misery.

“Sokka? Sokka!”

Sokka hiccuped a bit. Everything was blurry and awful.

“Get over here, you idiot, I can’t reach through the damn bars.” There was some grunting and a light tug on his sleeve. “Sokka!”

Sokka was confused, he was upset and he was hurting and he just wanted to be left to die in peace. “No,” he slurred. His tongue felt heavy in his mouth.

“*Yes*,” That was Zuko, no one else could pack that level of aggravation into one word. Sokka blinked his eyes open. Zuko had rammed one shoulder through the bars to grab at Sokka's arm and was attempting to pull him closer. “We need to get the wounds seen to quickly, so move your arse over here*.*” He punctuated his words with another tug although he was too far away to do more than grip the material of Sokka's shirt with his finger tips.

“I don’t think I can move,” Sokka admitted.

“Don’t be a moron, you have legs don’t you? So *move* them.”

He did indeed have legs and currently they were informing him that they were in complete agony and might fall off at any moment. Sokka whimpered, fully aware he was being pathetic. Somehow he forced his knees to unlock, and stumbled a few steps towards Zuko.

“Good. I need you to take your shirt off first, okay? I have to look over the injuries and get them clean.”

“No,” Sokka moaned. The idea of trying to get his shirt over his head was not an appealing one.

“Yes, do it now.” Zuko's tone was firm, but his voice wasn’t raised. In fact he sounded calm, possibly as calm as Sokka had ever heard him. It was freakish.

Reluctantly, Sokka began attempting to struggle out of his tunic, but the muscles in his back and arms were screaming with each movement and pain was exploding all over his body. He couldn’t do it.

“Come here.” Zuko took hold of his shirt through the bars and awkwardly pulled him out of it. The air hit his back in a rush and the breath left his lungs in a gasp. Why did everything feel so disjointed and fuzzy?

“I’m going to undo your pants and get them off you,” Zuko said. It wasn't a request, but Sokka appreciated the warning. He shut his eyes allowing Zuko to undo the fastening and tug them down. The irony of the fact Sokka had just offered to do the same thing for him only an hour or two ago was not lost on him, even in his current confused state.

“Step out of them,” Zuko instructed. “And lie down on your front.”

Sokka opened his eyes. Zuko had laid his shirt out on the floor of the cell as best he could. He was kneeling next to it, pointing with one imperious looking finger and blinking up at Sokka, his face stiff like he was trying to hide his concern behind indifference. As usual, he was doing a piss poor job at it.

Lying down was hard, but once achieved it was better, movement made everything worse. Of course the downside was that being still meant there was less distraction and the pain was less dispersed. It felt like it was building to some sort of crescendo, and when it reached its peak he might just split apart and explode because his body couldn't contain it anymore.

There were quiet voices, Zuko and a guard perhaps, but Sokka ignored them and closed his eyes again. He knew he should be embarrassed by the crying and the generally pitiful way he had been conducting himself, which had to be the least manly display of anything ever, but he just couldn't quite muster the emotion past the pain and foggy misery, and instead just lay on the cell floor like a fish waiting to be gutted.

He started back to awareness as he felt something soft on his face. With a jolt he realised Zuko was wiping the snot and tears away with a light cloth. It should have been humiliating, but instead he found it calming. It was such an unnecessary, human act, it centred him and brought him back from the panic and whatever place he had gone to in his head to escape from the shock.

“Am I dying?” he croaked. His voice sounded horrible, somehow both hoarse and wet.

“No, dumbass, but I need to treat your back. It’s going to sting,” Zuko told him, still with that freakish calmness.

“It's already stinging!”

“Well it's going to sting *more.* Now stop moving.”

Sokka rested his cheek on his arm and watched as Zuko heated a brass bowl of water until it bubbled. He jumped when flames started licking over Zuko's fingers, running across the skin and making it look pink and sore. Sokka realised he was sanitising his hands as best he could. That was nice of him. It would really suck to survive this and then be taken down by some basic infection.

“This bit is going to hurt,” Zuko informed him.

“I would rather it didn’t,” Sokka couldn't help but whine in return.

“Tough.”

Sokka sighed heavily into his arm. “Your bedside manner is shit, you know that?”

“I don’t care.”

Then there was a horrible searing, throbbing pain as Zuko firmly rubbed the cloth over whatever mess his back was in. Despite Sokka's best efforts, some more tears escaped, but he managed to not cry out or shame himself further. He was quite proud of that, as he felt like screaming until he lost his voice completely. “Talk,” he demanded, “Distract me!”

Zuko reheated the water and dabbed painfully at his back. “I don’t have anything to say.”

“Well, sing to me then,” Sokka ground out between gritted teeth. Zuko was awful at this, it *hurt*. “A song to carry me off towards my death.”

“I’m not singing, and you’re not dying.”

Still so horribly calm. Why wasn’t he yelling? Sokka wanted him to yell. “You’re a terrible disappointment to me,” he said.

Zuko grunted. “I’m a disappointment to everyone, you’re not special.” He moved on to the back of Sokka’s thighs and the pain was intense again as he cleaned the wounds with a firm hand. “You’re lucky, not all of them broke skin. I’m going to lift this up.”

Sokka realised ‘this’ was his loincloth when a rush of cold air hit his backside, but he was hurting too much to be embarrassed. There was a quick exploratory touch pulling at his skin, and then his underwear was gently set back in place.

“The extra padding protected you. It will hurt to sit for a few days, but the skin isn’t broken, so there is no risk of infection and you won’t scar.”

There was a joke there somewhere that Sokka really wanted to make, but he just didn't have the energy to figure out exactly what it was. And he wasn't sure if Zuko actually understood jokes anyway. “How bad’s the rest of it?” he mumbled instead.

“Not as bad as it could be. I’ve seen the damage the cane can do, you’re lucky.”

Sokka mustered just enough strength to be affronted. “You keep saying that, but I don’t feel lucky, I feel horrible! Distract me, Zuko!”

“I don’t know how!” Zuko snapped, then took a deep breath.

“I told you, sing to me!”

“*I’m not singing*!” Ah finally some temper, that felt more normal, safer. “I’m going to put some salve on the open cuts. It should help to numb the rest of the hurt too,” Zuko grumped at him.

Sokka couldn’t help the whine that escaped as Zuko started to apply the cream. *Spirits* he was heavy handed. “I bet you could sing if you tried,” he groaned.

Zuko sighed loudly and paused in his brutal ministrations. “I could recite to you?”

“Recite what?”

“Um, a poem? Or a play?”

“You memorised a whole play?” The next application of the salve had even more force behind it and Sokka winced, perhaps winding Zuko up while he was tending his wounds was not the best plan.

“Yes, I’m not an idiot, I *can* do things.”

“Like memorising plays?” This was an unexpected speck of joy in an otherwise bleak day, it was so odd and entertaining that it gave Sokka a little renewed vigour.

Almost as if he was sensing Sokka's thoughts, Zuko's voice took on a very defensive note. “Not just plays, useful things too, reports and maps and stuff. I remember a lot of the things I read.”

“Bet your teachers loved you,” Sokka grumbled, still amused despite the situation. He was momentarily distracted by the image in his head of tiny child Zuko, all angry and intent on learning to write, with ink on his face.

Zuko made a noise that was half sigh and half growl and wrung out the bloodstained cloth. “Not really, I was terrible at my lessons. My tutor said it would go in one ear and out the other. He said… well he didn’t have anything overly positive to say.” He tugged on Sokka's arm. “Sit up.”

Sokka attempted to sit, it was slow going, but the salve had worked wonders, numbing and easing the sharpness down to a low ache. “Well, dusty old scrolls are probably tedious for a child to learn,” he offered soothingly. His butt hurt when he sat on it, but it was a bruised pain, and manageable in comparison to the exploding agony of the rest of it.

Zuko was glaring at the bandages in his hands, clearly thinking about the past rather than the present. “I *liked* the scrolls, it was the lectures I couldn’t…I don’t know, I guess I just never paid any attention.” His fists tightened on the cloth in his hand, like he was throttling which ever snooty tutor had insulted him.

Sokka felt sort of bad for him, he was getting the distinct impression that Zuko’s childhood had not been a particularly fun one. “Maybe they were just teaching boring stuff?” he suggested.

Zuko transferred his glare to Sokka, “The history of the Fire Nation isn’t *boring*! Our ancestors' great deeds are legendary.”

Even when Sokka wasn’t trying to get a rise out of him, he seemed to rile him up. Oh well, may as well go all in. “Sound’s boring to me. I bet it’s all, tedious monologue, set people on fire, then more monologuing, probably about colonizing or fire, or murder, or fiery murder.” He sniffed. “*Dull*. Recite me a play.”

“No!”

“Please? I’ll try not to die and it will take my mind off the horrific pain. Did I mention the pain? Because it’s significant and I am two minuets from screaming again. And I’m pretty sure I’ve done enough of that today.”

“Okay, if it makes you shut up!” Zuko shoved some bandages through the bars at Sokka. “I can’t reach all the way around you, so you’ll have to help with this.”

“Alright.” Sokka awkwardly attempted to wrap the bandages around his front, while Zuko carefully did the back.

“Put your clothes back on, you’ll get cold.”

Sokka did, wincing and gasping as the motions pulled at the many points of hurt on his body. This was a really strange situation, Zuko helping him tend his wounds. More than that, he was trying to *comfort* him, in a strange grumbly way. “So, where’s this play you promised me?”

“I’m thinking!”

“Will you do the voices? I think you need the voices to make it work.”

“No. Now lie back down and shut up.” Zuko cleared his throat and Sokka had to fight back a smile. “Long ago, when the world of the spirits was close enough to touch our own and the shadows lay long across the land, there was a woman who lived alone by a sacred river...”

Zuko's voice had taken on a rhythmic cadence, he was *good* at this. A lot better than his efforts at being a healer. Sokka shut his eyes letting the words wash over him in a soothing wave.

Sokka woke with pain flaring over his body. He couldn't believe he had slept at all. But he supposed it was his body's reaction to the shock. He lay for a moment, collecting himself. The memory of the flogging was hazy, but the result was still very much making itself known to every nerve in his body. It felt more manageable now, and he thought perhaps the shock and fear had made everything worse before.

He missed Katara so, so much. Of course some healing would be amazing right now, but it was more than that, it was her gentle hands, her stubborn will and even her loud outbursts. Zuko was a poor substitute, but he had made the effort, and Sokka appreciated that a great deal, even though he was still a little surprised by it. Carefully moving his head, so it didn't set his back off again, he looked at his cellmate: Zuko was asleep beside him on the other side of the bars. His cheek pillowed on his arm and his mouth hanging open slightly. With his stubbly head he looked very soft and silly, even though he also looked awful: pale, and gaunt with dark shadows under his good eye. Sokka supposed he must look the same, except also blotchy from crying like a baby. A horrified rush of memory hit him, apparently now the pain had lessened slightly, it was time for the shame to set in.

He considered sitting up, decided to risk it and reached a hand for the bars to help. He had to take a moment for a few dizzying waves of pain to wash over him and recede like a fiery tide over his skin. His head felt weird. Light. Alarmed he reached up to inspect it, and found his wolf tail missing. He must have made a noise, as Zuko shot up into a sitting position, his eyes wild and darting around the room like he was expecting an attack at any moment.

“My hair!” Sokka said, stupidly. Considering the situation, and everything he had lost and was likely to lose, it seemed a silly thing to hurt him so much, but it cut him to his core, worse than the horrible pain he had just experienced. Something in his chest felt weak and wobbly. Zuko was looking at him with understanding and sympathy. He knew exactly how it felt, and Sokka was suddenly very glad he had listened to his conscience and not mocked him over his own unwanted haircut. It took quite an effort to hold back tears and he bit at his lip trying to keep them at bay.

“Are you okay?” Zuko asked, quiet.

“Do I *look* okay?” Sokka sniffled slightly.

“Not really.”

“You’re supposed to lie and tell me I look fine.”

“You look like someone that just got flogged and has been locked in a dark room for more than a week.”

“Why did he take my hair!” Sokka wailed, unable to hold it in any more.

Zuko shrugged, “Probably the same reason he took mine, mostly.”

“To prove I’m dead?”

“No, stupid, to send to your father to prove he has you.”

Oh spirits, that was a horrible thought. “It could be anyones hair,” Sokka protested.

“It will make him doubtful, he will know it *might* be yours.”

Sokka couldn't bear to think about his dad's reaction to receiving that, how much it would frighten him.

And under that, the selfish feeling of shame, for being caught, for being *here* and not protecting his village or even Katara.

So, he did what he did best and deflected. “Why’d Mutton Chops send your hair to your dad anyway, what does that prove? Surely it would have been better to send the whole head?”

Zuko gave him a flat, pinched look. “We could be weeks from the Fire Nation, it would take too long to get there and be…gross.”

“You could attach it to a hawk.”

Zuko looked at him like he was an idiot. “That wouldn't work, it would be too heavy for a hawk.” He fell silent, with a weird look on his face.

Sokka wasn't sure if he was calculating how to achieve transportation of a severed head, perhaps on multiple hawks? Or if he had just realised they were discussing how best to send *his* head, specifically, to his dad and was freaking out about it. Perhaps this was not the best distraction technique for them. “Time for another game!” Sokka announced, his voice ridiculously loud in the stagnant quiet of the cell.

Zuko jumped, then scowled. “What is it with you and childish games? I’m not playing I Spy again, it’s stupid.”

Sokka smiled, I Spy had been fun. But he didn't think he would be able to coax a repeat performance out of his cellmate, not yet at least. “Something else then. Do you know Rock, Paper, Scissors?”

“No,” Zuko looked amusingly suspicious.

“Come on, surely everyone knows this one. You shake your fist,” Sokka demonstrated, wincing a little as the motion pulled the painful muscles in his back. “And then you make a shape with your hand, rock.” He made a fist. “paper, and scissors.” he demonstrated again. “And you see who wins. Rock beats scissors, scissors beats paper and paper beats rock. Easy.”

Zuko shuffled forward a bit, “I know a different version of this,” he said. “This one doesn't make sense. I don’t understand how paper beats rock.”

Sokka rolled his eyes. Why did everything have to be an argument? “It covers rock, so it wins.”

“Yeah, but the rock’s still there, isn’t it? So how does the rock wearing a paper hat mean it’s been beaten?”

A hat? “Does it even matter?” Sokka sighed.

“Yes! It doesn’t make sense!”

“Spirits above and below, fine, what’s the version you know?”

“Mayor, Hunter, Fox.” Zuko made the appropriate hand motions.

“Fox? How is the fox involved? I assume it kills the mayor? How does that even work? You’re complaining about rocks and paper, but you have a fox defeating a guy? How?” Sokka demanded. The audacity of questioning his very sensible version of the game and then presenting *this* alternative? Unreal.

Zuko looked offended. “It tricks the mayor.”

“It’s a *fox*.”

“Foxes are cunning tricksters!”

“In stories, not in real life! You can’t complain about my version not making sense when yours doesn’t either!”

Zuko had that pinch-mouthed expression on his face and it lightened Sokka's heart a little. He never got tired of being the cause of that disgruntled exasperation. “Tell you what,” he said, leaning back a little to rest his weight on one hand, mindful of his back, and wagged a finger in Zuko’s direction. “Why don’t we invent our own version?”

“One that isn’t stupid?”

“Yeah, one we can both agree with.” Sokka looked at the ceiling, pondering.

“We could use the elements?” Zuko suggested after a moment of contemplation.

“Good idea! So water puts out fire...” Sokka said, while Zuko looked indignant at this literal fact of life. “And fire… cracks earth? But then how does earth kill water?”

“It could turn it to mud?”

“But water erodes earth, and earth can put out fire too, if you pour enough on it. So basically fire is rubbish.”

“Fire is not rubbish! It could turn the water to steam, and if it's hot enough, melt the rock,” Zuko insisted.

He really was so easy to get a rise out of, Sokka thought he had it down to a fine art. “That’s like volcano hot, and nothing can beat a volcano. Anyway, we’re leaving out air.”

“It doesn’t work with four things!”

“Well, it’s just like the Fire Nation to screw over air, isn’t it?”

Zuko made a face and huffed.

“Don’t roll your eyes at me, you people killed a whole nation! How do you even do that?”

“There was a comet...”

“I *know* there was, what I mean is, how does anyone just *do* that? Murder a whole people? It’s incomprehensible to me that someone ever decided that was something they should just *do*.”

Zuko looked a little uncomfortable. As someone who presumably had a conscience under all the Fire Nation-ness and yelling, perhaps it didn’t sit that well with him either. “How did they do it, anyway?” Sokka asked. “I mean, I know they harnessed the death comet, Roku told us that, but how’d they get them all? There must have been people not at the temples at the time of the attack? Some must have run.”

Zuko looked at his hands where they rested in his lap, like they were suddenly very interesting. “There were, or so I understand it, but Sozin hunted them down.”

Sokka shook his head. “And you’re okay with that idea?”

Zuko breathed out heavily through his nose. “It wasn’t that simple, the Air Nomads had an army, they were going to try to take over the world.”

Sokka threw his hands up in the air and waved them, momentarily lost for words. Even though the motion made pain flair all over his body, he was too incensed to care. “So what, Sozin the Fire Prick had dibs?”

“They were a threat to the Fire Nation! And the other nations too!”

Sokka wished there were no bars between them and he hurt less, so he could shake some sense into his thick skull. “Ever been to the air temples, Zuko?”

Zuko scowled at full force, it pulled at the scar and made him look like some sort of vengeful spirit in the dull firelight. “*Yes*, I’ve been to all of them.”

“See evidence of any army apart from your own?”

“They were cleared, laid to rest.”

“Well, you missed some in the Southern Temple, and let me tell you, there was no army except those in red.” Sokka was suddenly very angry, not with Zuko, not really, he was angry that the Fire Nation was clearly teaching its children lies. That shouldn't be a surprise, but it was somehow still shocking.

Zuko opened his mouth, but Sokka cut him off, furious, “And even if it was true, which it’s *not*. What excuse do you have to hunt down the rest? Those living outside the temples?”

“To stop them rising up against us, to prevent a rebellion!” Zuko's voice was hot too.

“Even the babies? An army of babies?”

“When they grew up...”

“So that’s an excuse to kill infants? Because they might one day grow up to take revenge for the murder of their entire people? *Might*?”

Zuko didn't seem to have an answer for that. Good, let him think about it, what it *actually* meant. “You let me know if you come up with an argument that legitimises baby killing. I’ll wait.” Sokka turned his back, letting his anger burn alongside the pain in his body.

Zuko failed to make a good argument for that, or indeed any argument at all.

A stretch of time of perhaps an hour, let Sokka's anger cool into misery. He was at the mercy of these people. It was hard to remember Zuko was one of them, sometimes. He wasn’t the epitome of the evil Fire Nation murder machine Sokka had taken him for, but he *was* still Fire Nation. He clearly still loved his country and his people, even though they had cast him out and abandoned him, even though they had hurt him. But that was something Sokka could understand. He had not wanted to believe that people of other nations could be bad, but folks like Jet, and that arsehole old man that had betrayed them in Haru’s village, and countless other people on their travels had proved otherwise. He had only wanted to believe the best of his own people too, but the way Master Paku, and chief Arnook had treated Katara and Yue and even G*ran Gran*, had shown him the ugly reality. It wasn’t the same as burning babies, but it was still pretty depressing. He groaned, hating the morose flavour of his own thoughts.

“Are you in pain?” Zuko asked, voice low, and equally miserable.

The thing was, despite everything, Sokka didn’t think he was a bad guy at heart. Deep down under all the shouting and growling, he did seem to give a crap to some extent. He cared about his uncle at least, and Sokka too, in a weird ‘we’re in jail together’ kind of way. Perhaps, instead of dwelling on the bad in people, Sokka should take a floaty leaf out of Aang’s book and think about the flip side – about the people who had sheltered and helped them, and about the fact some of those people had been Fire Nation, like the old guy from Roku’s temple, who was probably dead because of that hog-monkey, Zhao. Ugh.

He sighed, and shuffled round to face his cell mate again, who was looking somewhere between guilty, concerned and angry. “Yes, I am in constant pain, thank you for asking. And I’m pissed off, I’m going to have to sleep on my front. I’m not a front sleeper, I’m a back sleeper.”

“You snore less when you sleep on your front,” Zuko said. Perking up a bit at the opportunity to be insulting without mentioning the big, genocidal, camelephant in the room.

“I do not snore!” Sokka decided to give him a break, just a small one.

“You do. I don’t know why you’re bothering to argue the point, you can’t see or hear yourself when you're asleep, can you?”

“Are you watching me sleep, Zuko?”

“Yes.”

“Why are you watching me sleep!”

“What else is there to look at!” Zuko made a wild gesture with both hands. Dramatic much?

Sokka snorted. “Fair point. Fine, let’s try the rock paper scissors game again. What three things can we use that don’t involve paper hats, foxes or genocide?”

Zuko winced, but then brought a hand up to rub his chin, clearly applying his tiny brain fully to the problem. “Um, hunter, soldier, animal?”

“Okay, yeah that could work. Hunter.” Sokka made a shape using his little finger and thumb that looked like a bow, “Soldier.” Two fingers straight out as a sword. “Saber tooth moose-lion.” He made a vaguely toothy animal form.

“So the hunter kills the moose-lion, but why can’t the soldier kill it too?”

Sokka made a distressed noise. “Because the hunter has a bow! The soldier would have to get too close and the Moose-Lion would kill him!”

“Okay.”

“He agrees! Finally! Has anyone ever told you that you are a very difficult person?”

“It might have been mentioned once or twice,” Zuko said somewhat wryly. Sokka found himself grinning a little in response.

“So now we have a game that makes sense!”

“Wait, why is the soldier trying to kill the hunter?”

“You came up with these! You’re doing this on purpose!” He had to be, he was a surprisingly sneaky bastard, with a surprisingly sneaky sense of humour. But Sokka would not be outdone or out logiced. “The hunter, is in fact a poacher, and the soldier has to kill him for breaking the law.”

“….That makes sense.” Zuko nodded.

“I know, everything I say makes sense,” Sokka preened, and Zuko snorted at him with what Sokka liked to think was affection. “Ok, on three.”

They shook their fists three times and made a shape, Sokka went hunter, Zuko went moose-lion.

“Haha! I win! The Rock of Victory remains mine!” Sokka crowed.

Zuko made a face. “Again.”

They shook, and once again, Sokka was victorious, he was just too good at this.

They played on, and Sokka found that as with I Spy, he was winning far more than losing, probably because Zuko had a tendency to go for moose-lion more often than not. Sokka did lose sometimes though, maybe one or two out of every five rounds, which kept it fun. Unlike with I Spy, Zuko didn't appear to be frustrated with his losses, instead he seemed to be concentrating very hard, ignoring Sokka's boasting at his far superior number of wins, and carefully studying him like he was a confusing bug with too many legs.

Then, after maybe half an hour of Sokka basking in his repeated glory, Zuko relaxed a bit and started winning.

And winning.

And winning.

Until he was far surpassing the amount of wins per round that Sokka had been getting, it was uncanny, and unfair.

“How are you cheating at this!” Sokka demanded after his fifteenth straight loss “How!”

“I’m not cheating.” Zuko bounced the Rock of Victory on one palm, his lips twitching a little. The bastard was *enjoying*this, which while only fair, was also infuriating.

“You are!” Sokka fumed. But he couldn't work out *how*. He was definitely doing something. As annoyed as he was, Sokka's curiosity was even stronger than his competitiveness, and eventually he just had to ask. “Okay, I give up, tell me how you’re doing it, I have to know.” He made some beckoning motions with his hands.

Zuko rolled the Rock of Victory over the tops of his fingers and considered, then he shrugged. “People do things unconsciously when they want to make an action or a choice. If you fight, especially with hands or swords, which I don’t, but if you did, then you have to learn to recognise an opponent's intention, and you have to do it quickly.” He shrugged again and leaned back on one hand, still toying with the little lump of coal. “The same goes for fighting benders, but the nature of bending means those moves are more telegraphed because of the forms used.”

“So you watch for the little things that are giving away what I’m going to choose? And that works?”

“Not every time, and not with all people, but yeah, if your opponent is obvious, which you are.”

“I resent that remark.” Sokka thought about it some more, he liked the idea that this could be applied to fighting in the real world. “So if you were a weak bender, you might be able to overpower a stronger one, if you were better at figuring out what they were going to do before they did it?” he asked.

“Potentially, yeah. But it wouldn't be easy, you would have to be very good, know your opponent well and be lucky, I would think. The man that…there was a swordsman I knew, that I saw beat a master firebender. It takes more than just observation, but it is definitely possible.”

“Was that master you?” Sokka asked, teasingly.

Zuko snorted “I’m far, far from a master. It was my uncle, actually.”

“And random sword fighters are just allowed to defeat imperial firebenders?”

“When they’re friends, yeah.”

“And this man taught you how to sword fight?”

“I don't use swords. But if I did, I would have gone to him to teach me.”

“Did he teach you to do the observation trick?” There was probably enough plausible deniability in that statement for Zuko to accept it, despite his ludicrous insistence in sticking to the no swords story when it was clearly untrue.

“He didn’t really need to teach me that, he said I was just naturally good at it.” He looked down and away, fidgeting with the Rock of Victory.

There was definitely something there, and Sokka was desperate to dig it out. But he was pretty good at people, and he could see they were skirting close to something uncomfortable, and Zuko tended to explode and withdraw if he was pushed too far over some topics, usually the family or honour related ones. “Can you teach me?” Sokka asked, hopefully.

“I don’t know. I’ve never tried to teach anyone anything before.”

“How hard can it be? I’m a great student, and we have nothing but time.”

Zuko tapped at his chin again, that seemed to be a thing he did when he was thinking. See Sokka could do this already!

“Okay, well I guess you need to do two things, firstly, become aware of what *you* do, that might be telegraphing your move, and don’t overthink it,” Zuko said.

“So wait, you could if you were really clever, construct a lie from this? You know, like if you pretended always to twitch a finger before you went hunter, then you could use that to trick someone into making the wrong move at a critical moment?”

“Yeah, I guess you could do that. I never really thought of it like that before.” Of course he hadn’t, he probably couldn’t lie with his body any more than he could lie with his mouth.

“What’s the other thing I have to do?”

“Just watch me, face and hands usually, but with swords you also might look at the shift of a person's weight, or a change in their breathing.”

“Won’t me learning this stuff disadvantage you if we ever have to fight for real? When we escape from here I mean.”

Zuko snorted, rudely, in Sokka’s opinion. “I’m not worried about it. I’m pretty good…*hypothetically*, if I used swords, then I would be pretty good with them, and I’m sure I could squash you like a dung-fly-beetle.”

“I don’t know what that is, but it doesn’t sound flattering.” Sokka said, with some indignation.

“It’s not. Practice, *now*.”

“Bossy,” Sokka grumbled. But he settled back to do as he was instructed.

They practised and played until they were bored and the pain in Sokka’s back was too much for his continued concentration. Zuko offered to reapply the salve, and carefully worked the bandages off. He still lacked any sort of delicate touch, and it felt like he was trying to scrub the wounds away rather than treat them, but the slave was soothing after it was smacked onto the skin, so there was some relief at least.

“Did you treat your wrists?” Sokka asked as he shrugged his shirt awkwardly back over his head and went to work on wiggling into his pants without making his legs feel like they were going to fall off.

“Yeah,” Zuko sounded miserable again, like he did whenever he was reminded of just how shitty the Fire Nation was.

“Do you think it will help with the infection? Or are we going to have to deal with you getting sick?”

“I cleaned it, this stuff is good. It’s what we use to treat burns so it should prevent infection, as long as we look after the wounds and keep them clean.”

It wasn't easy to keep anything clean in the cells, including themselves, they both stank, but Sokka was so used to it by now, he barely noticed it. The astringent odour of the salve was enough to override the regular smells of sweat, fear and chamber pots, and it made for a nice change.

Sokka knew that things were only going to get worse the longer they were here, they *had* to get out. He leant his head against the bars separating them, the metal cool against his skin. “Have you ever heard the phrase, ‘*the enemy of my enemy is my friend?*’” he asked, pensively.

“Yeah.”

“Well, I think it applies in this situation, don’t you?”

Zuko was quiet for a moment, still sitting cross legged and straight backed about a foot from the bars. He stared at Sokka with an inscrutable expression on his face. “You’re suggesting an alliance?”

“Yes, just until we get off this boat. I assume you want off this boat?”

“I want to be as far away from this fucking ship as it is humanly possible to get,” Zuko said, with feeling. “So, yes.”

“We need to come up with an escape plan.”

“We do, but first, we need to figure out what to do when they come back. He’s not going to leave you alone, he *will* do it again.”

Sokka shuddered. He didn’t think he could take that again, the thought of more hurt on top of the pain he was already in was terrifying. “Spirits,” he muttered.

“He said if we give him information, he might give you a break.”

“We? He’ll accept it from you too?”

“That’s what he implied. I feel…I feel like he’s just playing with me, making me wait before it's my turn, I don’t know what he wants from me.” Zuko’s lashes fluttered as he looked away.

He was scared too, the thought made Sokka feel stronger in himself and he felt his spine straightening. He was a warrior of the Southern Water Tribe, a people that apparently made the Fire Navy nervous enough to interrogate teenagers over scraps of information. He was a protector, as well as a fighter, and if Zhao frightened Zuko, then Sokka would have to be doubly strong for both of them. “That was my impression too. He’s playing a game, but I’m not sure what the rules are.” He scratched at his short hair and winced at the feel of it. “I guess we need to decide what *our* rules are. Do we give him anything? Or do we just hold out?”

“I don’t think I have anything much to give, but what I do have, he will have to pry out of my cold dead hands,” Zuko snarled. No surprises there.

Sokka thought about the pain, about the fear and about the Water Tribe prisoners, their bodies lost to the ocean. “Agreed. We say nothing.” He held out a hand and Zuko stared at it. “You’re supposed to clasp my arm. We’re agreeing to escape together, and not telling Zhao *shit,* whatever happens.”

Zuko reached out his own hand and gripped Sokka’s arm. His fingers were hot and rough with calluses. Probably from all that sword fighting he didn’t do.

“Allies, then,” Zuko said. “I will not reveal anything to Zhao or his men, unless it is so agreed between us. I will lend aid and protection where I can, and make every effort to get us both off this ship to safety. So I do swear.”

Of course he had to be *dramatic* about it. Sokka held back his smile, keeping his own face as solemn as Zuko’s, and cast his mind back to the wording he’d used to Taiju, when promising to return to the bars of his cell. “I swear it will be so,” he said.

Zuko’s good eye widened a fraction, then he made the sign of the flame and bowed, not exactly low, but *much* lower than he had when bargaining with Zhao. Sokka returned the gesture a little awkwardly.

Two weeks ago, he would have called anyone who trusted in Zuko’s word a moron, and told them they deserved anything they were going to get. But everything was different now, he *did* trust it, in fact it was one of the few things in this stinking ship that he was sure of.

In spite of the looming fear of Zhao’s return, he felt lighter, like there might be hope.

That was probably a mistake.

## Chapter 7

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

“Your crew taught you how to steal shit, is what you’re telling me?” Sokka said, delighted.

#### Chapter Notes

Please read the warnings for this one, folks!

Warnings for this chapter: Violence, threat, forced nudity – nothing overtly sexual happens, but stripping and forced nudity in this sort of situation is inherently an act of sexualized violence, so I’m going to give a blanket warning of non-consensual situations for the next few chapters. Nonsexual nudity between the boys (it is 100% a non sexy situation)  
Also brief discussion of the deliberate and cruel killing of a pet cat.

Many thanks to my beta burkesl17, who has managed to wrangle my dyslexic nonsense. All mistakes are my own.

### Text

Sokka woke with a scream on his lips. He sat up, wincing as pain flared across his back and legs, he was gasping and the residue of his dream felt like it was clinging to his skin. Beside him, Zuko’s hand was stretched through the bars like he had been about to try to shake him awake.

“Are you okay?” Zuko asked, his good eye wide and alarmed.

“Fine,” Sokka gasped between gulps of air. “Super fine.”

“Okay,” Zuko said doubtfully. He belatedly pulled his hand back.

“Nightmare,” Sokka added pointlessly. He pulled himself into a more comfortable position, resting his sore back against the wall as lightly as possible. They had taken to sitting side by side with only the bars separating them. Sokka supposed the proximity was comforting, which was a very strange thought when he explored it too closely.

“Was it the flogging?” Zuko asked, as blunt as a rock to the face as usual, and apparently determined to make everything worse by making Sokka *talk* about it.

“No,” he blinked away a rush of images and lingering fear. “It’s always the same dream.”

“Do you want to talk about it?”

No, he did not. Sokka grimaced, but Zuko plowed on regardless, perhaps not picking up on the obvious signals. “My uncle always says a problem shared is, um, a problem doubled?” he paused and frowned. “Wait, that’s not right.”

“’A problem shared is a problem halved,’ is the idiom you’re looking for.” Sokka felt a smile try to force its way out in spite of the lingering fear pumping itself through his body.

“Whatever,” Zuko said a little sullenly.

“It’s not really a dream, I mean, it is, but it’s a memory,” Sokka said, taking a few deep breaths. “It’s the same every time I sleep, but this one was just...” *Worse, more real.*

“Go on.”

“The first night we were here, Zhao lined up the prisoners and Chen took them out one by one and murdered them.” Sokka had to pause to fight down a rush of nausea, his hands were shaking. He wasn’t sure he wanted to talk about this. “He picked them randomly out of line, and each time I thought it was going to be me.” He let out a shuddering breath, his whole body was reverberating with the remembered terror. “In the dream, I’m kneeling on the deck, and I can feel the blood soaking through my pants, I can feel the cold on my skin from the sea spray, it’s so *real*. Then they pick me out, and I wake up just before the blow lands.”

Zuko was doing his blinking face, processing his words, his hand seemed to have risen up again, without his knowledge, hesitating in the space between them like he didn't know what to do with it. After a long moment, he reached through the bars and patted Sokka on the knee. It was clear he had no idea how to offer the comfort he wanted to give, but it was such an awkward, sweet and absurdly funny gesture in light of the horror of Sokka's words, it genuinely warmed him. “Thanks, buddy,” he said.

Zuko looked unsure how to respond so he withdrew his hand and put it back in his lap.

Sokka had added the slow, painful passage of time to the things he hated most. It ranked after the officers on the ship, the Fire Nation in general and the pain he was in, but it was pretty high up the list. Between bouts of fear, everything was just so crushingly boring and bleak. He hated his own thoughts spinning in his head, and the oppressive silence. Waiting for something to happen was intolerable. It was worse, because he both *wanted* something to happen to break up the tedium and the relentless nothingness of waiting, but he also knew that if something *did* happen, it would undoubtedly be something Very Bad.

He was also concerned by Zuko’s obvious anxiety, his eyes kept wandering to the door of the hold, like he was expecting Zhao or Ro to come marching back down for round two. This did not settle Sokka's nerves, in fact it was doing pretty horrible things for his blood pressure.

When the door opened for a guard to deliver their food, they both jumped and Sokka thought he might actually puke from the rush of adrenaline that shot through his body. Zuko looked like he was having the same issues from the slightly grey tinge to his skin. Luckily, it was just their hardtack, this time served with a small nub of slightly wizened, preserved ginger.

Sokka took a bite as soon as it was in his hands. It burned on his tongue and he chewed it slowly, reverently. It tasted like *something,* a sensation he had almost forgotten the feel of.

“Not that I’m in any way unhappy about it, but why’d you fight so hard for this?” He asked, savouring his second bite as much as the first.

Zuko gave him a confused look. “If you’re at sea for a long time with only dry rations like ships biscuit, you get sick. The Water Tribe must spend time on the ocean, surely you have something similar?”

“Not really? We probably don’t spend so long just at sea. Before the Fire Nation attempted to wipe us out, we used the ships for hunting and fishing. But we don’t have dry rations like this, there’s not much in the way of grain. Plants in the summer, sure, and we preserve some of them for the winter, but mostly we live on what we catch. We save that too, obviously, so if the winter is hard no one goes hungry.”

“Huh.” Zuko looked thoughtful. Sokka wondered what the Fire Nation taught its citizens about other cultures, nothing good, probably. “What sort of weapons do you use to hunt?” Zuko asked, he seemed genuinely curious.

Sokka nibbled on his remaining ginger and launched into a detailed description of the first summer hunt he had been allowed to go on with his father and Bato. He spoke about the animals he had seen, how his dad had taught him not just about how to catch and kill, but also how to break down and preserve the carcass. He veered into slightly distressing territory when began talking about the things they had eaten on the trip and he had to suck the spit back into his mouth when he started to salivate at the memory. *Spirits*, but he was hungry. He wasn’t the only one, as Zuko’s mouth was hanging open slightly and he looked completely mesmerised at the description of a simple summer stew.

When Sokka had torn himself away from describing food, he had explained a little about how village life worked, Zuko asked questions, but they tended towards his confusion regarding the chief taking part in what he considered menial tasks, like hunting or building, and not just sitting at home being waited on hand and foot. They clearly had a long way to go for Zuko’s understanding of people that were not his own, and existed under completely different conditions. Sokka tried not to show his contempt for his cellmate’s limited world view. It was hardly fair, considering he himself had held similar ignorance on the world outside his own borders before beginning his travels. Of course, he had been learning and changing, whereas Zuko had spent three years outside the Fire Nation and did not seem to have learned anything at all.

Except Avatar related stuff, *that* he had learned.

After they lapsed back into silence, Zuko apparently digesting Sokka’s stories (or perhaps just thinking whatever nonsense thoughts rattled around in his thick skull at such times) and Sokka was left with a home sickness that made him ache. He could feel his anxiety rising again, sick and churning. Ignoring the feeling did not make it stop, so he decided to try examining it a little closer – he found it seemed to mostly consist of an ugly mix of fear and shame.

He felt ashamed of being so frightened, he had spent the past few days trying to convince himself he was not afraid of whatever would happen next, trying to fake his way into being strong. But the truth was he was terrified. Afraid of the pain that might be coming his way, afraid of shaming himself by crying or begging. He was scared of having to watch the same thing that had happened to him happen to Zuko, or of having to watch him die. All the talk of his home and his family had reminded him of Zhao’s plan to tell his father he was being held captive. The thought of his dad receiving his wolf tail, what he would *feel,* and what would it mean for them.

On one hand, if his father believed that the hair was his, someone would know he was alive, and rescue might actually be a possibility. But on the other, surely Zhao was aware of that? He would be ready for it, expecting it even. Would his dad fall into such an obvious trap?

He didn’t think so, but what if he *did*? What if he died because he was trying to rescue his son? Sokka wouldn’t be able to bear it, couldn’t live with it.

But then, what if his dad *didn’t* come? What if he didn’t even try?

Intellectually, it would make more sense if his dad saw this as a trap and stayed away. It was the right choice – his responsibility was to the tribe, not just the village, but *all* the Water Tribes, even the Earth Kingdom, in a way, as he was involved in their fight as much as their own. He couldn’t throw that away to rescue one person, even if it was his child.

But Sokka just wanted his dad. He just wanted to be free of this constant fear and be safe in his fathers arms, even if he was too old for that. He wanted it *so* bad.

Zuko seemed to have fallen into a light doze, head tipped back against the wall. But Sokka couldn't make sleep come.

He was finally just slipping into a dream when the door banged open and Zhao and Ro entered the hold. Ro being there was not a good sign, if that bastard was involved, it meant something horrible was about to happen. Just as alarming, a handful of other soldiers followed them into the small space. That probably meant they were expecting a fight, and that meant they were here for Zuko. Even half starved he had more muscle mass than Sokka had ever archived in his whole fifteen and some years. But even he was looking a little gaunt now, both of them were underfed and out of shape. They just weren’t getting enough to eat to waste the energy on exercise. Sokka’s brief attempt had resulted in him feeling sick and shaky in five minutes flat, and had also resulted in an annoying lecture from his cellmate regarding pushing his body beyond its limits, which was infuriatingly hypocritical considering Sokka had witnessed Zuko throw his body into fights and off buildings without any apparent consideration for *limits*. He was such an irritating person in every possible way and Sokka didn’t want to see him die.

He rose to his feet as Zhao approached the bars, beside him, Zuko did the same.

“Good morning,” Zhao greeted them pleasantly.

Ro brought out an apple and took a bite. Sokka stared at it, he could hear the crunch, and watched, fascinated as a dribble of juice ran down the man's chin. Now this? This was torture. *Crunch*. Spirits he wanted it, he could feel spit start to gather in his dry mouth at the thought. He tore his eyes away to check on Zuko – he was glaring at Zhao, tense and ready. Sokka didn’t think he was going to go easy with whatever they had planned for him.

The silence was feeling oppressive, so Sokka decided to step up. “Good morning,” he offered, his tone as polite as Zhao’s had been. Beside him, Zuko made a huffing, snorting noise.

“How did you find the ginger?” Zhao asked him.

“Tasty. We could use a little more, maybe some fruit, or dried meat, if you’re offering.”

Zhao's lip curled into a smirk. “That does sound nice, although this is hardly the environment for a picnic.” He brought a hand up to his face to cover his nose. “The smell in here is not pleasant.”

Zuko snorted again, louder. “Give us enough water to wash with then, and clean clothes.”

Sokka couldn’t help but wish he wouldn't rise to the bait every single time. The bastards were playing games again – there was a direction they wanted to take the conversation, and Zuko fell for it without fail.

Zhao looked pleased with the predictable reaction. “Is that so? An interesting suggestion, one I might consider, in part at least. I do think wearing the same clothes for such a long time is probably not very healthy. Take them off.”

That was not what Sokka was expecting, and he didn't like it one bit.

“No,” Zuko looked confused at the demand. “You give us fresh clothes and water to wash and we’ll change.”

“I did have a feeling you might say that. No matter.” Zhao nodded to the soldiers waiting by his side. “Strip him.”

Zuko's body shifted into what Sokka recognised as a fighting stance. Shit, this was going to get ugly fast. There was nothing he could actively do to help and he found himself taking a couple of steps away from the bars. If people started firebending he wanted to be able to doge.

Zuko remained where he was as the three men entered the cell. There wasn't much room to manoeuvre with four people in such a small space, which Sokka thought might give Zuko a bit of an advantage as they couldn't all attack him at once. But they were all grown men who had probably come from eating a nice breakfast, and he was a teenager that had been living on practically inedible biscuits made from some sort of horrible sawdust. Sokka had seen Zuko fight, and had been on the receiving end more than once, although he had usually been just trying to get Sokka out the way so he could get at Aang. Who knew he would feel nostalgic for being kicked in the head by a Fire Nation prince? Anyway, he knew Zuko was good at fighting, he just wasn’t sure how good at hand to hand he was. So Sokka watched the proceedings with mounting concern.

Zuko stayed in his loosely held stance, he seemed poised on the balls of his feet, ready to move. He remained still though, until one of the men lunged for him, then he lashed out with a quick motion, moving his hands fast and sure. There was a sickening *crack* of bone as the soldier leapt back with a howl, cradling his wrist which was hanging at an unpleasant angle.

Zuko was quite good then, Sokka resisted cheering, not wanting to tempt fate. The other two guards looked a little less happy to be in the cell with him now.

Part of that might have been the smell. Zhao was not wrong in his comments about that.

Zhao shook his head in mock disappointment. “What did I tell you about attacking my men? That’s one punishment. I strongly recommend you do not incur another.”

“I strongly recommend you treat us properly,” Zuko shot back. “Bring us water and clothes and we will happily clean up.”

Yeah, this was going to go very poorly. Sokka was now an expert in Zuko behaviour and he could always be relied on to do two things. One, react to threat with aggression, and two, be stubborn enough to put even Katara to shame, and she was the most obstinate person Sokka had ever met in his life. This was only going to end with them losing, and it was probably the sensible thing to just do what they were told, even if it was humiliating. But that just wasn't going to happen, was it?

The two men lunged for him together, and Zuko lashed out again, spinning on a heel and catching one of the guards a powerful kick to the jaw with the other foot, the man went down. Zuko spun again, narrowly dodging a punch from the remaining guard. They both stepped back, eyeing each other carefully. The other guy had a similar stance, ready to move, he was not a huge man, but he was bigger than either of them, fit and well fed. If it came to a prolonged fight, Zuko was going to lose no matter how stubborn he was.

“Admiral,” Ro said, “If I might suggest?”

“Please go ahead, lieutenant.” Zhao looked amused by the two wounded guards rather than angry, he was *pleased* by Zuko's violent response. Sokka didn’t like it.

“If he’s going to cause trouble, we could start with the other one. Maybe take a fingernail or two.” He nodded towards Sokka, who backed up further from the door. What was *wrong* with these people?

“No!” Zuko snapped, still keeping an eye on the man in his cell. “Your fight is with me not him. You want me to strip, you come in here yourself and make me, coward!”

Zhao ignored him. “Good idea, lieutenant. Perhaps that is the way forward.”

“No!” Zuko said again, stalking deliberately at the guard in his cell, who was glancing at Zhao and rapidly backing up, as if unsure if he was supposed to fight or just remove himself from the path of the infuriated teenager who had nearly kicked his buddy’s head off. Not receiving any further instruction from his superiors, the guard grabbed his unconscious friend by one arm and dragged him out the cell. Ro stepped forward and shut the door as Zuko reached the bars and smacked them with an open palm, making the metal clang.

“Feel lucky it’s just a couple of nails, next time you refuse we'll take the whole finger,” Ro told him.

“Fuck you!” Zuko was steaming slightly, he was so enraged, but it seemed to just be a subconscious response to his emotion. He wasn’t aggressively bending, so hopefully it didn’t count as breaking the rules.

Ro went to the door of Sokka’s cell and Sokka stared at him, feeling a little like a rabbit watching a polar dog bear down on it. His feet were frozen to the floor with horrified fear, and not just for himself. He wondered if Zuko had realised this was scripted, that this was intended from the start. They were testing the waters of the relationship between the two of them, looking for the weakness and Sokka was pretty sure they were going to find it. In many ways it would be better for them both if Zuko just continued to fight and let Sokka suffer whatever unpleasantness was coming to him. Because if he didn’t, then he was leaving himself wide open to much greater hurt later on.

On the other hand, excusing the unfortunate pun, Sokka was rather attached to both his fingers and his nails and was not in a hurry to be parted from them. Neither choice seemed optimal.

“Okay!” Zuko snarled. “Fine, you want this so much, *fine*.” He tore his shirt off and threw it to the floor.

And there it was.

The expression on Zhao’s face was pleased, not because Zuko was doing what he was told, but because he was doing it to prevent a threat to *Sokka*. Ro paused with the key still in the lock and glanced at Zhao, who gestured for Zuko to continue.

“Your word you won’t hurt him?” Zuko asked. The muscles in his back were bunched with tension under the impressive array of ugly, faded bruises covering his skin.

“Given.”

Zuko stripped off his pants and undergarments and chucked them at the bars like he wished they were some sort of flaming projectile rather than stinky grey rags. Then he stood straight and solid in the centre of the cell, crossed his arms over his chest and glared. If Zhao had been hoping for any sort of cowering or show of humiliation, he wasn’t getting it. Sokka felt a feeling of fierce pride rise up in his chest.

Ro looked annoyed at the defiance and the lack of any obvious fear, but Zhao’s expression was carefully neutral. “You too, Water Tribe,” he said.

Sokka considered fighting, but really what was the point? With some considerable reluctance, he stripped off his own clothes, shivering in the cold air as it hit his skin. He dumped them by the bars and then mirrored Zuko's stance. He wasn't going to let them see his discomfort, fuck that, as Zuko would say.

This time he definitely saw Zhao's lip twitch. “Very good, that wasn't so hard was it? You can have some extra water to wash.”

“Very kind of you,” Zuko snarled. “And clothes, do we get those or do we just have to sit here in our skin?”

“Those you will have to earn, Zuko.”

“Earn *how*?”

“Water Tribe, you will give me something useful. Zuko, you will agree to pour my wine when I eat my meals.”

Oh great, they were going to be naked forever. Zuko had gone very red in the face, his flush spreading down his chest. He was shaking with rage, Sokka thought he could actually see him vibrating in place, he was so angry. But he didn’t move or stop glaring, although it looked like his jaw was clenched so hard he might crack a tooth.

Zhao lifted a finger. “There was also the matter of punishment, you attacked two of my men. I told you what would happen if you did that, didn’t I? I’m not an unreasonable man, from now on you can choose - you can take it yourself, or the little water rat can take it for you. Up to you.”

“I can handle my own punishment, Zhao, you sick fuck.”

“And watch that tongue with me, or I will cut it out. Do I make myself clear?”

“So, punish me then,” Zuko snapped, transferring his glare to Ro, who smiled, slow and full of anticipation. This was clearly the part he had been waiting for. He stepped into the cell and approached Zuko with a casual sort of caution – well earned in Sokka’s opinion. He stopped, far to close and smiled again, a wry twist of his lips.

“You’re not going to attack me, are you?” he asked.

There were waves of hate coming off Zuko, making the air seem thick with it, but he made a dismissive noise, sucking at his teeth, and resolutely stared forward not paying Ro any more mind than he had to. “I said I wouldn’t. Get on with it, unless your plan was just to bore me into submission?” he said.

Ro punched him in the stomach, making him grunt and bend double, gasping for air. As soon as Ro withdrew his fist, Zuko straightened into the same arrogant stance, breathing through the pain. Sokka suspected that it was taking every ounce of willpower not to punch back. Standing still to just take a beating form someone like Ro, for whom he held nothing but contempt, went against his nature. Ro struck again, and Sokka looked away, although it was impossible to escape the sounds of impact and the occasional pained grunt. Zuko's refusal to cry out was making Sokka squirm in shame thinking of his own complete breakdown.

Zhao was watching with another of those smug looks. He was toying with some sort of bendy wooden stick. Sokka didn't like the look of it, but then, Sokka didn’t like the look of Zhao in general, he hated him in a way he had not thought himself capable of. He hated the expression on his face, he hated standing naked in front of him, he hated the way he looked at Zuko and the calculation in his gaze. He *hated* him. But he wasn't going to underestimate him – the man had a game plan and Sokka was well aware they were falling into line exactly as he wanted them to. Unfortunately, as things stood now, he couldn't see any way out of the situation, and he didn’t think Zuko was going to be able to stop himself reacting when nudged toward certain ingrained responses. Sokka was honestly surprised he hadn’t kicked Ro in the head yet.

Once again, unable to do anything but watch, Sokka stood naked in his cell, arms still crossed over his chest and glared at Zhao. He had been practicing his glare, based on some of Zuko’s best – it lacked the same drama, with him not having half his face burnt off to give it that extra level of zing, but he was pretty sure it got the message across.

Zhao wasn't looking at *him* though. He tapped the sick on the bars. “Enough, lieutenant.”

Sokka turned back to the other cell. Zuko was on his knees, panting, red marks covered his back and sides, they looked painful, but superficial. Sokka thought he had the measure of Ro, he wasn't someone to get lost in violence, he enjoyed it too much. He preferred precise, painful strikes, not likely to cause permanent damage. Sokka thought he might hate Ro as much as he hated Zhao, which was quite a lot of hate to cope with on a daily basis.

“Get up,” Zhao said. Still smug, although his voice was cool.

Zuko got up, pushing himself to his feet and returning to his defiant posture, if a little more wobbly.

“That was for the kick you gave Ota. Now, for the broken wrist. Come to the bars and put your hands through, palm up.”

Zuko made another aggravated noise and strode stiffly to the cell door, putting his hands through the bars. Zhao stepped forward with his little stick. It whistled as he struck Zuko's palms, making Sokka shake with his own remembered pain. The stripes on his back, almost forgotten in the adrenaline of the new danger, flared back to life, sending shivery pulses of hurt though his body.

Five strokes and Zuko didn’t make a sound.

Zhao used the end of the stick under Zuko’s chin to tip his head up at an awkward angle. “You attack my men again, and I will really hurt you. Both of you. I recommend you remember that. You will not get another warning.” He stepped back. “Now that unpleasantness is over, I will send some extra water down so you can wash. This place reeks. If you want clothes, something substantial to eat or any other concessions, you know what you have to do, don’t you?”

Neither Zuko or Sokka responded, and they remained on their feet until the soldiers had all left. Zuko’s posture relaxed a touch as the door shut behind them. “Shit,” he said, succinctly.

Sokka was feeling a little dizzy, he felt exposed and off balance, which he supposed was the point to this whole song and dance. He just wanted to curl up and hide from these awful people and this horrible place.

Zuko returned to his usual spot and sat, wincing a little, presumably at the cold of the metal floor. He flexed his hands, examining the damage. That was something Sokka could help with, it would give him something to focus on. “Do you need salve?” he asked.

He was feeling far more awkward about the lack of clothes than Zuko seemed to be – he had just plopped himself down crossed legged with his usual straight backed posture like nothing had changed at all, apparently unconcerned with being naked now the other men had gone. But then after you had taken a crap in front of someone a few times, nudity didn’t really have the same level of intimacy as it might have before. It was strangely comforting and lessoned Sokka's own confused anxiety a little. Gathering the shreds of his own dignity, he sat in his usual place, and nearly screamed - the floor was *freezing*!

Zuko was still examining his palms. “No, the skin’s not broken,” he said.

“But it still hurts, right? The salve numbs the pain a little.”

“It’s fine.”

Sokka waved the salve at him threateningly. “Just give me your hands, jerk.”

Zuko made a face at him but moved himself forward a little and stuck a hand through the bars. The lines across his palm was angry and red and the sight made Sokka shiver again in memory.

He rubbed a little of the salve over the wounds. Zuko didn’t really need someone to do this for him, he could easily do it himself, but the act of helping was restorative, and Sokka liked to think he was a lot better at it than his heavy handed cellmate. The guy treated injuries like he was trying to beat them out of existence. Actually, come to think of it, he probably was.

“How’s the rest of you?” Sokka asked. “Looked like Ro got in some hits.” He was going to be a mass of bruises on top of bruises in a few days.

“It’s fine. I’ve had worse.”

“Sure, but...”

“Just leave it. We need to decide how to deal with this.”

Sokka let go of Zuko's hand and tucked his knees up, resting his chin on them. “Deal with it? I don’t know, does it change anything?”

“No, at least not for me. Fuck him, he just wants to humiliate us. But he’s going to have to try a lot harder.”

That was kind of what Sokka was worried about. “Zuko, they didn’t even ask any questions. He didn’t even ask anything about my dad. He must know it's pointless, but then why do this? Why do any of it?”

“It’s just an excuse, they know we have nothing for them.”

“Yeah, I *know* that, but why play these games?” Sokka drew in a deep shuddering breath.

“Because they can, I guess. Ro enjoys it, Zhao does too, but in a different way.”

It was that ‘different way’ Sokka was becoming increasingly concerned about. Ro liked causing pain in a manner than was more than casual, he seemed *aroused* by it, which was a horrifying thought in a multitude of ways. But Zhao wasn't interested in the pain aspect, it was humiliation and the power it brought him that he was enjoying. Why else would he tell Zuko he had to pour his wine of all things?

Making a prince, even a disgraced and disinherited one, serve your dinner table was…well, it was a lot and it made something in Sokka’s stomach knot up. He didn’t like the shape Zhao’s behaviour was taking. He wasn't sure how to talk about it with Zuko, or even if he should. They couldn't stop what was going to happen to them, so why make him worry about it? As far as Zuko was concerned he was just going to sit naked in this cell until the world burned down around his ears. No way he was going to give into Zhao’s demands.

Except, he *would*, with the right leverage. And Sokka had a very strong suspicion that leverage was going to be *him*.

He needed a distraction from his thoughts before he did something embarrassing like scream or cry. “You and Ro know each other, I take it? He seems to personally dislike you.” Not the best conversation starter, but at least it veered away from the Zhao… issue.

Zuko sighed and flexed his sore hands again. “He was on my crew, when I first left home. I was not given the… best of the bunch to command. But they weren’t all bad. I learnt some interesting things from them.”

“Like how to cuss like a sailor?”

“Yeah, like that. Also how to, um...” he looked suddenly abashed.

“You can not leave it hanging there, my friend, like what? Did they get you drunk?” Oh that would be a funny sight. If they ever got off this murder boat, he would have to try to live long enough to get Zuko drunk. Not that he himself had ever actually had more than a few sips of alcohol in his short life. But he was sure it would be entertaining.

“No!” Zuko said, his eye wide. *Blink blink blink*. There was a lie there! Sokka gave him a long look.

Zuko let out a very put upon sigh. “Only once. And it wasn’t so much they gave me the liquor, it was more like I helped myself to it.”

Sokka chuckled, it was a little weak, but he couldn't muster the level of enthusiasm he might have before today's events. “And? Did you fall overboard?”

“No. I fell down the stairs and dislocated my shoulder, then I threw up on myself and decided to never do it again.”

Sokka snorted, “Your story telling could use some work, that’s not the sort of wild tale I was hoping for. What else did they teach you? Did they take you to a house of ill repute?”

“A what?” Zuko looked perplexed.

“A brothel.”

“No! Why would you even…? No!”

He looked genuinely appalled at the suggestion, Sokka tried not to laugh. “No? Pity, that would have made an entertaining story – what was it then?”

“Nothing like that. Just some skills that I might not have learned otherwise.”

“Like…? Come on, you have to give me more than this,” Sokka coaxed.

“Some hand to hand combat, which I mostly learned from watching them brawl. And a little, um, sleight of hand type stuff. Picking locks. You know.”

“Your crew taught you how to steal shit, is what you’re telling me?” Sokka said, delighted.

Zuko flushed. “Yeah, pretty much.”

“Can you pick this lock?” Sokka pointed toward the cage door.

“Not without a tool of some sort, I can’t just use my fingers.”

Okay, but if they could get something somehow then they might be able to get past the door, which was good news…but for that to happen, they would have to get to somewhere that was not the cell in order to procure a tool. So, pointless. “We’re getting off track. What happened with Ro, he was on your crew?”

“He had been…let go from his previous post for something my uncle refused to discuss with me. Whatever it was, my uncle didn’t like him, and he wanted rid of him.”

“And I take it he did do something that got him the boot?”

“I had...we had a cat on board, most ships do, to deal with the rats.”

Zuko’s face had fallen at the mention of the cat, he looked so sad and regretful, Sokka felt bad for him, and for bringing this up. “You and the cat were buddies?” he asked.

“Yeah. Her name was pancake.”

“Pancake?”

“Yes,” Zuko said, defensively.

“What happened to her?”

“Ro threw her overboard and watched her drown. It was a clear day, so it...” Zuko clenched his jaw, clearly still upset. Sokka didn’t push, he could infer: it took some time for the poor thing to die. He felt his own anger rise up. Stupid to get mad because of a long dead cat, rather than their own situation, but he couldn't help thinking about how Zuko must have felt. That he had taken to this animal because he was lonely, and on his own on a ship full of adult men.

“Your uncle acted against him then I hope?”

“Ro denied it, but a crew member said they saw him, and he had scratches on his arms. It was enough.”

“Why does he blame you for it?”

Zuko shrugged. “He hates me because I punched him in the balls, I couldn’t reach his face.”

Sokka tried very hard to smother his grin at the mental image, but Zuko didn’t notice, too busy glaring at his hands again.

“It wasn’t much in the grand scheme of things, but he held a grudge I guess, for the shame of being kicked off the worst ship in the fleet and for being punched by a kid in public. But I suppose he got passed on to Zhao, and he fit in much better on this crew.”

“Yeah, no kidding. They’re like two peas in a pod, those two.”

“Like what?”

“Like two...never mind, they’re very similar is what I mean.”

They sat in silence for a while. Sokka couldn't help the twist of fear that was sliding through his guts. He had always known there would be no reasoning with these people, but somehow in the back of his mind he had still been kidding himself he could appeal to their humanity in some way. But he couldn't. There wasn’t any to appeal to in the first place.

“It’s good you didn’t give him the satisfaction,” he said at last.

“What do you mean?”

“You didn’t show them your pain, or cry or anything.” Sokka squirmed, he was regretting starting this conversation already.

Zuko was looking at him with a steady expression “There’s no shame in crying when they flogged you,” he said. “I’ve seen hardened soldiers weep after receiving the lash. It’s supposed to hurt and people cry when they hurt. It’s involuntary.”

Sokka wasn’t sure about that, *he* hadn’t cried when he was being beaten and having his hands whipped. But he did appreciate the sentiment, and Zuko really did seem like he meant it. “Did you ever order someone to receive a flogging when you were on your ship?” he asked, curious.

“No, but I oversaw it. Discipline was up to my first lieutenant. I could have stopped it, if I thought it wasn’t merited, but he was a fair man, he didn't punish people for no reason, and never to any sort of extreme. Never more than fifteen lashes. Although for something really bad, a man might get two sets, with a break to heal in between.”

“The navy sounds like it sucks.”

“Sometimes. But if you’re stuck on a ship at sea, you have to obey the rules, if you don't, your actions could lead to the death of the whole crew. Discipline is essential. If you did something dumb like drinking on watch and got flogged, would you do it again?”

“No. I take your point. Although I’m also not convinced hurting people is the most efficient form of discipline.”

Sokka shivered. The air in the hold was always cold, but now without even the scant protection of clothes it was going to be doubly unpleasant.

Zuko looked him over critically, making Sokka want to squirm. “Sit with your back to the bars,” he instructed.

Sokka shuffled round leaning his back against the metal and wincing at the cold. It was not altogether comfortable, but was just about manageable with his head cradled in the space between the bars. He heard Zuko move too, and then there was a small rush of heat all up his spine, it was glorious. The back of Zuko's head knocked against his own as he mirrored Sokka's position. The air between them heated again, just enough to stop his shivering. “I can’t do much more than this at the moment,” Zuko said apologetically.

“Nah, this is great. You never seem very cold, do you people always generate more heat than normal folk?”

Zuko sniffed. “Firebenders tend to run a bit hotter than non benders, not like you, you’re cold like a wet fish.”

“Rude. So when Aang learns firebending, will he suddenly start being a portable heat source? Like, will firebending change his body?” Sokka asked, his mind leaping onto a subject that was not horrible suffering in a boat.

“I never thought about it,” Zuko said, he also sounded a little curious.

Sokka could imagine he was doing his pondering frowny face. “You never think about anything interesting, so that's not surprising,” he said.

“Well you never think of anything practical! How will this help?” Ah, now it would be annoyed frowny face.

“Got anything *more* interesting to think about?”

“Escaping!”

“And how’s that going for you? Any plans suddenly manifest? No? I’m shocked.”

Zuko grumbled something rude under his breath, and Sokka gave himself a point for that. “But seriously, is the body temperature warmer from birth? Can you hold a baby and be like, oh yes this is a hot one, going to be a nice little ashmaker when it grows up?”

“Shut up, Sokka. I’m trying to *think*.”

“About baby ashmakers?”

“*No*!” Zuko made a frustrated growl of a noise and fell silent, presumably into a sulk.

Sokka drew his knees up and hugged them close, trying to conserve as much heat as possible. He wasn’t sure if he should broach the subject of the fact Zhao was going to use them against each other, should they lay ground rules? Was there a limit before they would give in? *Should* they give in? How much was Zuko’s pride actually worth to him? What if it was a choice of who would have to suffer something really awful?

“Zuko?”

“What?”

“I wasn’t totally honest about my dream earlier.”

“Why not?”

That didn’t feel like the right response to the question, and Sokka snorted lightly. “Because I was ashamed, and afraid.”

“Okay. You don’t have to tell me anything.”

“I know, I want to. I told you about how each man would be pulled out of line, how it happened in real life?”

“Yeah.”

“Fact was, every time Chen pulled out a new person, all I felt was relieved, *so* relieved it wasn’t me. And it’s like that in the dream, every time it’s someone else I feel dizzy with relief. Every time Chen is walking down the line I’m thinking, *pick him, not me, pick him.* What kind of person does that make me?”

Zuko took a moment before answering, thinking his words over. “A normal one. Someone who doesn’t want to die. Someone who didn’t deserve to be in that position in the first place,” his raspy voice was low and sad.

Sokka decided not to tell him that he had joined the line of Water Tribe prisoners in the dream, how Sokka had watched him bleed out more times than he could count, and how every time was devastating. “It makes me feel inhuman, far, far from *normal,”* he admitted. He wasn’t sure he remembered what normal was.

“The situation you’re in isn’t *normal*.” Zuko went quiet again. Sokka could almost feel him turning something over in his mind, clunky cogs spinning. “I’m sorry, for what happened to you, and for what happened to the prisoners. It was wrong.”

“It’s hardly your fault,” Sokka admitted, “I mean, you’re right here beside me, aren’t you?”

“I’m beside you, but we're not on the same side. I love my people, I love my nation. I want us to win this war.”

Sokka snorted, “Well they don’t exactly love you back, and I don’t know what you think you’re winning other than hatred, but go on.” Harsh perhaps, but the boy did not understand subtlety.

“I am aware,” Zuko snapped, then took a deep breath, a lovely little wave of heat from his body accompanied it. “Perhaps win is the wrong word, I would like the war to end, and I don’t see how else it can be achieved. But then I look at people Like Zhao and Ro...”

“The cat murdering bastard.”

“Yeah, fuck him. But I see people like them, and what they are doing, and it seems to me it’s not just hurting the other nations is it? It’s hurting our own too.”

Okay, there might have actually been a little progress there! Sokka was proud. “Look Zuko, I definitely want you guys to lose this war, and I think your dad is a dick and I would very much like to poke him in the eye, preferably with a very sharp knife. But I’ve met a few people from the Fire Nation who have been good, or you know, just people, doing people things.
