And I think this war is hurting them just as much as it’s hurting the Earth Kingdom folk, or the Water Tribes.”

“I don’t want it to be like this!” Zuko half growled and half yelled. “I don’t know why it is!”

“As I see it, it’s like cancer and Zhao is a symptom of the disease. We can cut it out, but Sozin was the source of the original tumour and it’s had a hundred years to fester, spread and grow new pockets of illness, and they spread their own little tumours.”

“Then we have to keep cutting it out,” Zuko insisted, with a strong decisive note to his voice. “I won’t let these people poison the good in my nation! I *will* remove it.”

Sokka let out a surprised burst of laughter and Zuko took the lovely heat away from his back to turn around and glare at him. Sokka shifted around so he could look him in the face, unable to keep the smile down. “You know what? In your own weird, aggressive way you’re almost as optimistic as Aang. We’re naked, in a prison hold, on a boat, who knows where, and about to suffer who knows what horrors, and you’re like, *I’m going to defeat the cancer ruining my nation!*And you’re saying it with *conviction.* You are completely balls to the wall insane. I love it.”

Zuko gave him a grumpy, obstinate face “What would you rather I do, give up?”

“No! My money’s on you, I don’t think I’ve met anyone as tenacious and stubborn.” Sokka laughed again and shook his head. “The way you just stood there earlier, stark naked and still staring them down, kind of made me proud to stand beside you.”

“Well, we have an alliance,” Zuko said dubiously, like despite the praise he still wasn’t sure if he was being made fun of or not.

“Yeah, we’re a team! We need a team name.”

Zuko sighed loudly and went back to leaning against the bars. “We do not need that.”

“We do. Fire and water, oh! Team steam!”

“No.”

“We are team steam, we are the steamiest! We should make a secret handshake.”

“*No*, Sokka.”

“I will wear you down. You may be stubborn, but I’ve lived with Katara for fourteen years, I *know* I can do it.”

“Can’t.”

“Can too.”

“No, Sokka!”

### End Notes

#### Chapter End Notes

Thank you for all the comments and kudos! I treasure them all ❤️

## Chapter 8

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

“Oh yeah, that went super well. Your control of your temper was exemplary. Lasted what? Thirty five seconds? Forty?”

#### Chapter Notes

Please take care in this one folks! Mind the warnings and the tags.

Warnings: Panic attack, description of waterboarding, burning, flogging, violence, implied threat of non-con (physical and verbal) with some vague discussion around the subject.

Many thanks to my beta burkesl17, who has managed to wrangle my dyslexic nonsense. All mistakes are my own.

### Text

“Sokka! I need you to match your breath to mine, can you do that? Here, feel my breathing, in… and out...in...and out.”

Sokka couldn't get air into his lungs. He was suffocating, gasping, sucking great gulps into his mouth, but he couldn't get it to go where it was needed and his chest was burning, tears were wet on his cheeks.

“Feel my breath, in and out.” He could feel heat under his hand. A fast beat and then slower, rising and falling, up and down. He gasped and heaved and panicked, tried to focus on the movement.

“In and out.”

He attempted to slow his struggle for breath, *in and out*, following the rhythm under his hand.

“Are you back with me?” Zuko’s voice.

Sokka filled his lungs with air, focused on breathing, *in and out*. Zuko was holding one of Sokka's hands to his bare chest, breathing strong and steady, a bright point of warmth under his palm. “I’m okay,” he managed shakily.

“Take your time, just keep following my breath.”

The cell was swimming a little and Sokka slumped against the bars. He left his hand where it was, finding comfort in the touch and heat.

“They’ve gone,” Zuko told him. “It’s just us, you’re safe.”

“Not sure about that, buddy,” Sokka slurred. They didn't even have to do anything to him to completely bring him to his knees. He couldn't stand it. Zuko squeezed the hand he was still holding against his skin, his fingers were hot and rough and his grip was strong.

It took some time before Sokka felt in control enough to sit up properly and take back possession of his hand. Zuko stared at him with his eerie cat-snake eyes, and Sokka was vaguely disturbed to find the sight of them comforting.

It had been by their estimation, a full week since Zhao had taken their clothes. A week of Sokka feeling exposed and uncomfortable and Zuko being furiously defiant every time a guard entered the hold to provide food or just to gawk in an attempt at shaming them.

Sokka remembered those early days fondly, it had been four days since Ro had started trying to drown him.

“Zuko?”

“Yeah?”

“I can’t do that again, I can’t,” his voice sounded so pathetic, it made something shrivel up and die inside him. Three days, it had taken three fucking days for him to break. Today they hadn’t even bothered, as he had panicked at the sight of them - why bother drowning him when he could lose his breath all by himself?

When they had first come for him, he had thought they were going to flog him again, and he very much wished they had. The pain was more bearable than the unspeakable terror they had subjected him to instead. It was the simplicity of it that was so horrible. They had taken him out of his cell, strapped him to a chair, tipped it back at an angle, put a bit of stinky cloth over his face, while Zuko had yelled at them, then water had started seeping through it, and it wouldn’t *stop* and then he had been *drowning.* All thought had left his head other than a wild, unchecked fear, and he had fought and struggled and screamed and screamed in the back of his mind, but he hadn't had the breath to give it voice.

After, he had puked and snivelled. Zuko had attempted to calm him by telling him a story about a woman who had disguised herself as a man and gone to sea to hunt down the pirates that had murdered her father. He seemed to have an endless supply of epic poems, stories and short plays about women going on vengeful murder sprees. Sokka wondered if that was just his taste, or if he had learnt them from a woman who had empathised rather strongly with the heroines. If so, he was guessing his mother or sister. Sokka was betting mother, if he was married to the Fire Lord he would probably have a thing for righteous revenge murder stories too.

He had barely slept that night, and the next morning, they had come back and done it again.

They still hadn't asked him any questions.

Instead, hours later, Zhao would set up the table to eat his evening meal, sometimes alone, sometimes with Ro. He didn't speak to them, just ate or read through some scrolls. The smell of the food was a torment, even after the level of acute fear Sokka's system had just been subjected to.

He never asked if Zuko was ready to give up and pour his wine, just like he never asked Sokka about his father. He just ate.

But the message was very clear.

“Are you okay, Sokka?” Zuko asked again, breaking him from his thoughts, which were lodged somewhat confusingly between gibbering fear, shame and the roast fish that Zhao had been eating yesterday evening.

“I’m sorry,” Sokka said, he couldn't put into words what he was sorry for, he hadn't done anything *yet*. But he thought if he had to go through it again, he might just blurt out whatever they wanted to hear, whether it was true or not.

“Don't be sorry.” Zuko reached through the bars and patted him on his bare knee. It was still a funny gesture, given the circumstances, but Zuko seemed to have gotten used to the brief touch as an offer of comfort and did it whenever Sokka was in distress. It was sweet, weird, but sweet.

“I’m going to break,” Sokka admitted. He was shivering; from cold, from shock, from fear, who knew anymore?

“So let’s break.”

“What?”

“I’ve been thinking,” Zuko said solemnly.

“Don’t strain yourself.” The joke fell flat, what with how hard it was to get it out around his chattering teeth.

“We aren’t gaining anything from holding out, we’re only losing.” Zuko sniffed indignantly and pulled a frowny face, just for him. Sokka appreciated it.

“Won’t we lose if we give in too?” he asked.

“Yeah, we will, but we might be able to gain for later.”

“What do you mean?”

“We’re just sitting here while they hurt us.” Zuko looked furious. “While they hurt *you*. In order to change our circumstances we have to change them...I mean we have to do something different, in order to make things different.”

Sokka squinted at him, as he tried to unravel the messy sentence. “You mean we will never find a way to change things in our favour doing nothing?” he guessed.

“That’s what I said, isn’t it?” Zuko huffed. “Nothing will change if we just stay in here. If I agree to pour his wine, at least I’m out of the cell. I might find the opportunity to get hold of something to pick the lock.”

Sokka nodded, thinking. “It’s risky Zuko, very, very risky. Firstly, giving in gives him more power, secondly, if you lose your temper at him he might just kill you, and I cannot think of many situations more likely to make you blow your top than him making you serve him his lunch.”

Zuko made a face that was part distaste and part wry acknowledgement, “Yeah, I can’t say I’m thrilled by the idea. Not punching him in his stupid smug face will be hard. But...” He paused and looked at his hands, flexing them, staring at the faded bruises. “But if we do nothing, he’s just going to keep hurting us and we’ll never get free. We’ve been here for *weeks*. And we’ve not gained a single thing to use to our advantage.”

Sokka was inclined to agree, they had not managed to come up with any way of getting past the doors of their cells let alone getting off the ship to safety. He was good at plans, good at thinking creativity, but it turned out when you had nothing but cups and chamber pots, it was quite hard to move past the planning stage. The constant pain and fear were not helpful either. “So we negotiate?”

“Yeah, we may as well get everything we can.”

“Pants would be good,” Sokka said, resting his chin on his drawn up knees.

“I miss pants.”

Sokka felt himself smile a little. “We should try to get some food, we need energy if we're going to do any fighting, or any swimming, as we might have to go over the side while we're still at sea.”

“We need them to stop doing what they are doing to you, too. Perhaps we can think of something to tell them that isn’t important?”

“That would be nice, very nice.” Even the thought of having that cloth on his face made Sokka's toes curl in remembered terror. “But what? I don’t even know anything!” That was not strictly true, he knew where the fleet had been heading, Bato had provided him with that. But they probably weren't there now…but what if they were? He couldn't use it, too risky. But maybe he could cobble something together from it? “I could make up a place they were a few months ago. But they wouldn’t even be there now.”

“Too close to the truth, if they realise it’s a lie, they will get it out of you.”

Zuko had seen right through that, so it was likely Zhao would too. Sokka briefly felt a little anxious that Zuko now knew he might have some actually useful information, but he was surprised to realize he wasn’t actually that worried about it. Zuko was obsessed with his honour and keeping his word was a huge part of that, so their agreement to help each other would hold. Alongside that, or perhaps because of it, was a fierce loyalty which was now somehow directed towards *Sokka*, how did that even happen? Still, as intense as Zuko was, Sokka couldn't actually wish for a better person to be stuck in boat jail with.

“How about Avatar info? I could share some Aang facts of no importance?” he suggested.

“That might be the best option, but be careful, even innocuous information can be useful.”

“That how you tracked us all over the world?”

“Yeah. Small bits of information can be used to predict the most likely path you would take. I know a lot about all of you.” Zuko slid him a narrow look, a little amused.

“Like what?” Sokka demanded.

Zuko shrugged. “You prefer meat based meals, while the Avatar is a vegetarian, so the places you stop need to have options for both of you. Also, you like to try foods from everywhere, but if there is something that can be found in the Water Tribes, you and your sister tend to want to eat that. The Avatar has a thing for egg custard tarts and sweet fruits. He likes to waste time playing with animals that want to eat him or stomp on him, and if there is something ridiculous going on, like a festival to celebrate hog-monkey breeding season or a competition to grow the biggest bulb of garlic, he’ll want to stop. You like shopping, drawing and reading, and secretly enjoy poetry.”

Sokka opened his mouth to object, then shut it again when Zuko gave him a smug look. Zuko picked up the Rock of Victory, still in his possession after their last round of Soldier, Hunter, Moose-Lion and fiddled with it. He was not subtle at all, the arsehole.

“I know the trade routes the airbenders used most frequently, it makes sense the Avatar would take the paths he is more familiar with,” Zuko said. He bounced the pebble on his palm. “You get enough information, and you’re looking at a detailed map, you can start making educated guesses.”

Sokka was...impressed, he reluctantly realised he had to reevaluate Zuko’s intelligence once again. He did a lot of stupid shit and he was *weird,* his brain didn’t work at all like Sokka's. But he was smart, when he did actual thinking and not just reacting.

“What else did you discover?”

“Katara can’t resist trying to help people in need, I mean the Avatar too, but she’s the leader when it comes to getting involved in other people's problems.”

Accurate, however Sokka didn’t think he had ever mentioned his sister's name. “You knew my name the whole time didn’t you?”

“Obviously, I’m not an idiot. You lot are though, you weren't even trying to cover your tracks or hide who you were. Very stupid – people could use that to trace you back to your village and hold them against you.”

“*Shit*.”

“I wouldn't worry too much about it now. Things have changed, you’re too far away to bother with hostages from the South Pole. It was still pretty dumb though.”

“How were you going to use the information you gathered? Or were you just using it to track us?”

“Tracking mostly, it wasn’t that hard. You’ll be surprised to learn that people tend to remember a ten ton, six legged, flying cow passing overhead.”

“Oh shut up. And he’s a bison!”

“I did consider combining it to set a trap, if I knew roughly where you were going and attacked a village,” He held up a hand to cut Sokka off before he could comment. “Not to harm the people, just to imperil them a bit. I figured you would come to me. It never worked out though.”

Sokka rubbed at his forehead. “You get that ‘imperilling them a bit’ and therefore terrifying them, is harming, right? Like, that is still harm. Also, I don’t know if you have noticed, but peoples homes tend to become on fire when you and Aang face off. That is also significant harm.” How could someone be so bright and so dumb at the same time? It seemed to be an issue with foreseeing and understanding consequences outside things that would directly affect *him.* Actually, no, even then he still did stupid shit. Zuko was a very ‘act now, think later’ sort of person. Which probably explained why he was currently sitting in a prison cell awaiting death.

“I guess. But it would have worked,” Zuko muttered.

Sokka sighed. “Yeah, probably would have. But we would have escaped and defeated you, because we’re awesome.” Leaving another village burnt to cinders and people displaced and probably looking at a very lean winter. Perhaps it wasn’t just Zuko that wasn’t super great with consequences, but at least Aang was *learning* as he went.

“It only takes one mistake, Sokka, just one. I mean, look at us now.”

“Excuse you, but I think we both made a catalogue of mistakes to lead us to this place, far more than just one.”

Zuko leaned back against the wall and made a scrunchy face. “True. There were some disasters, and perhaps some choices that should not have been made.”

“Like committing treason to free the Avatar from your own nation?” Sokka smiled a little, to soften the jab.

“Yeah, that would be one of them.”

Sokka laughed. Well at least he was actually admitting it now.

Zuko fiddled with the Rock of Victory again. “I wasn’t trying to set him free, you get that right?” he said, gazing at Sokka intently. He only seemed to have two ways of looking at a person, fleeting flickers, avoiding direct eye contact, or staring at you with the force of an incoming gale. Being caught in it actually felt a lot like the time Sokka had nearly drowned in that stupid hurricane. Very similar.

Sokka stared back. “Oh yeah, I get that. Doesn't make any difference though, it was still treason.”

Zuko looked away and sighed grumpily. “If I hadn’t been shot in the head, I would have captured him and taken him to my father and none of this would have happened.”

“It’s a good thing you have a hard head. Look, I get why you did it, but I’m afraid I’m glad it worked out the way it did, even if it did lead to this situation. No offence.”

“None taken.”

“Because to me, Aang’s survival is more important than either of us.” He pointed a finger at Zuko and poked it at him a few times. “And even if you had captured him somehow, and I’m not convinced you would have been able to, you're a good bender, but Aang’s a master. And has the ability to become an all powerful force with creepy glowing eyes, he would have escaped. Overall, it was a terrible, desperate plan.”

“Terrible desperate plans is all I ever have.”

Sokka chuckled again. “Yeah, I see that. We might need another one of those soon.” He fell silent for a moment, thinking. This relationship was very strange and sometimes he felt shocked when he thought too hard about it, but it was also the only point of light in these dark few weeks, and he was very grateful for it. “Thank you for earlier. That was embarrassing, they didn’t even take me out the cell, I don’t know what came over me.”

“It’s panic. When the fear of the thing you’re scared of overtakes everything else.”

Sokka shot him a look, but Zuko was avoiding eye contact, his face turned to show more of the scarred side. “You were pretty good at getting me out of it,” Sokka said, casual, but gentle. That came from experience, no way this awkward moron just knew how to do that.

“My uncle showed me.”

“Yeah? How come?”

“When…after.” He pointed at his face. “After that, I had some problems.”

Sokka had noted after weeks of exposure, that Zuko had some trouble verbalising things at times, especially when talking about distressing or confusing subjects. He tended to either garble his sentences or reduce them down to single, stilted words, so they were almost like some obscure form of poetry that took a master to untangle. Luckily, Sokka was that master. “I can imagine. What triggered it? Fire? Bit awkward for a firebender.”

“It took a while to be able to bend. Took awhile to be around *candles*. My uncle helped. He did the breathing thing.”

“He sounds like a good man, when you talk about him.” And a patient one. Sokka reckoned the old man had earned every one of those grey hairs trying to keep his nephew alive. Sokka thought he might be going grey just worrying about him exploding and getting murdered in a fit of pique.

“He is a good man. I never told him that I thought that. He must think I’m dead, if word has gotten out about my execution.” Zuko rubbed his hands over his short hair and flopped back against the wall. “I hope he can find some peace with it.”

Sokka tucked his knees up a bit higher. “Yeah, I can’t help thinking about what my dad must be feeling if Zhao sent him that letter.”

“Maybe he’s bluffing. Maybe the letter never reached him and he doesn't know.”

“Yeah. Maybe.” Sokka didn’t think he was that lucky though.

They were leaning against the wall side by side with only the bars between them. Normally Zuko preferred to sit opposite him, unless they were sleeping - which they had taken to doing back to back to share what warmth they could, as well as those small points of physical contact where their skin touched through the bars, offering a level of comfort that Sokka had been unaware he was missing. He had never thought of himself as someone who needed or craved touch in that way, hugging hadn't felt like a manly thing to want, so he had avoided it and been fine, but now he realised he’d always had *Katara*, who was so free with her physical affection, he had never actually been without the comfort of warm human contact, he just hadn’t noticed it at the time. He ached at the thought of her.

The only problem with the current seating arrangement was Sokka could only see the scarred side of Zuko’s face, limiting how well he could read his expressions. In the moment of quiet he let himself look at the scar, the way it started at his eye and swept back almost into his hairline. He didn’t think it looked accidental, and he wisely decided against asking about it, but he couldn’t stop looking at it now he had started. The skin was red and a little shiny, a lot of the ear had been burnt away.

“Can you hear okay on this side?" he asked. He wondered what it felt like, if it was as smooth as it looked. Without thinking, he reached out to touch the burnt shell, but Zuko must have seen or registered the action and moved his head, so Sokka poked him right in the ear instead. While this was awkward and extremely rude, the high pitched squeak Zuko made was a sound that Sokka would treasure forever and startled a laugh out of him. “Sorry!”

“Why’d you do that?” Zuko snapped, scowl tugging at the edges of the scar.

“Sorry!”

“Why?” He seemed bewildered and offended and Sokka felt bad – apparently impulse control issues were catching.

“I don’t know!” There was actually no good explanation or excuse for that, Sokka decided to ignore it and plow on with his original question. “So, can you hear on this side?” he asked.

Zuko turned round fully to glare at him, and Sokka couldn't help staring at the burnt eye. The scarring had clearly restricted the movement in his face so he couldn’t open it fully, but the colour was slightly different too.

“Why do you care?” Zuko snarled. Clearly this wasn’t something he wanted to discuss.

“It might be important to know, if we’re escaping and I’m whispering or gesturing to you on the wrong side and you can’t see or hear what I’m doing.” A sensible and plausible explanation.

“Oh.” And just like that he settled again, blinking a little. “I can see movement and shadow out of the left eye and nothing else. I can’t hear at all out of that ear. I got an infection and it took anything that was left.”

“Sorry to bring it up, but it might be important later.” Somehow it was actually *worse* than It looked.

“It’s fine, I learnt to compensate, especially because of the eye. I had to relearn how to fight. It was hard, but I did it,” Zuko said, matter of fact.

“I would expect nothing less to be honest. Tenacity is one of your more profound character traits.”

Zuko didn’t look sure if he was being praised or insulted.

“I mean that as a compliment. Although I did not appreciate it when you were chasing us all over the world, I sure appreciate it now.”

Zuko had a funny, constipated look on his face, like he got when he was attempting to think before speaking and trying to figure out his words. “You talk a lot, but in a good way,” he said after a moment of intense contemplation, then winced. “I mean, when you talk you say things properly.” *Blink blink*.

Sokka took a second to enjoy the warm feeling that flooded him. The idiot was trying to return the compliment, and he was blushing red enough to match the scarred side of his face. It was *adorable* and Sokka was not completely sure how to cope with that fact. How in all the world had he ended up getting the warm fuzzies from *Zuko*? “You mean I have the gift of the gab? Or are you trying to tell me I’m very wise? Because both are true.”

Zuko frowned through the blush. “I wouldn’t go that far, I just meant you’re…articulate. You know how to say things when they need saying. Although you talk a lot of nonsense too.”

“Thanks buddy, I try.” As joyful as poking fun at him might be, it was probably better to give him a way out of his confusion before the sweetness turned into frustrated yelling. So he let it drop.

When they heard the key turn in the lock, Sokka reached through the bars to grip Zuko's shoulder briefly. “Zuko, you have to hold your temper, no matter what.”

“I will.”

Sokka wasn’t so sure of that, but he didn't really have a choice. “Okay, good. Let’s see if we can get through this and do something ‘different, in order to make things different.’”

Zuko made an annoyed pout at him and Sokka grinned, but he schooled his face to be as neutral as possible as they got up to welcome their jailers.

Zhao and Ro, lovely.

“Good evening,” Zhao greeted them. It was clear he knew something was up.

“We want pants and food,” Zuko said without preamble.

Sokka sighed. Maybe they should have discussed the idea of him doing the talking part, it would probably be less likely to end in bloodshed.

Zhao looked entertained by the blunt demand. “So you want to negotiate terms? That's very bold of you, since I hold all the cards in this situation.”

“You said we would have to give you something in return for concessions, so I assume you’re open to the idea,” Zuko insisted. He was glaring like he was hoping his hate alone might set the man on fire.

Sokka stepped forward, resisting rolling his eyes. “What he is trying to say, is that we would be open to hear your terms and make a deal, if you’re willing.”

“Are you speaking for him, Water Tribe?”

Sokka shrugged. “I’m speaking for both of us. We both have things we want from you, and you have things that you want from each of us.”

“What would you like from me?”

“Spontaneous combustion,” Zuko muttered, only just under his breath. He was definitely going to get them both killed.

Sokka attempted a friendly smile. “Clothes, some extra food, and I would quite like to not be drowned again, or flogged. That’s my number one, personally.” Sokka kept his tone light and friendly.

“I can imagine it hasn’t been a fun week for you,” Zhao said, equally affable.

“Not so much, no.”

“Well, you know my terms, you need to agree to give me information that I will find useful, and Zuko needs to pour my wine.”

Zuko made a low growling noise but managed to keep his mouth shut, which was a relief, although probably a temporary one.

“Okay. Can we get pants first?” Sokka asked hopefully. He eyed Ro who was smirking as he set out a few dishes of food and a jug of wine. It was roast fish again with some accompanying rice and what looked like salted pickles, perhaps of some sort of tuber. It smelt amazing and Sokka's mouth watered.

“Yes, I think I can allow that. If you behave, I will allow you to have some fish and dried fruit,” Zhao told him.

The idea of actual food was almost as overwhelming as the idea he might not have endure any more torture. Of course, it was a big ‘if’ and came with the extra concern that if Zuko *didn’t* behave he might get very seriously hurt. Sokka wasn’t sure if he was going to be able to do it or not, and by the conflicted look on Zuko's face, neither did he.

Having finished laying out the table Ro stepped forward and handed them both a pair of ratty red pants through the bars. They were loose and short, looking more like underclothes than anything else, but it was something, and not freezing his balls off on the metal floor would be wonderful. Sokka shoved them on the second they were in his hands, and although they were scratchy and unpleasant, they were blessedly warm. Not being naked was *amazing*. “Thank you,” he said, politely.

Zuko of course held Zhao's eye as he pulled his on, not giving an inch in his defiance. Zhao just smirked at him. There was an air of anticipation about him and it put Sokka’s teeth on edge.

Ro unlocked both cages and Sokka had a sudden moment of fear. What was he even going to tell them? What if they didn’t believe him and it made everything worse?

Speaking of worse; he watched worriedly as Zuko stepped clear of the cell. He was eyeing the set up like he was considering flipping the table and trying to stuff the whole fish down Zhao's throat. Sokka was slightly concerned he was actually going to attempt it, which would be something of a disaster.

“Okay,” he said, his voice going a little high in his anxiety. “So you want me to tell you things?”

“Sit,” Zhao said, pointing to the chair. And you,” he said to Zuko. “Stay where you are.”

Sokka was having a very bad feeling. This had been a huge mistake, he should never have listened to anything Zuko said, he didn’t think about *consequences*. Still, nothing they could do now except see how it played out. He sat, it felt strange to be so close to food, almost like he was sitting down to a meal. Although it would be the worst dinner party ever, with Ro standing far too close to him. Sokka had to take a few deep calming breaths.

Zhao had half an eye on Zuko, who was scowling by the door to his cell. Sensible not to turn his back on him, Sokka had a feeling if he did, Zuko might lose his cool and attack him just on instinct, there was a look on his face that was just one step away from feral.

“If your information doesn’t match up with what I already know, if there is any inconsistency, if you are lying or if I think you are just giving me something useless, you will be burnt. Do you understand?” Zhao said, almost conversationally.

Sokka nodded jerkily. He did understand, and he realised far too late that this was a trap. He had nothing that he could give that would satisfy the man, it didn't matter what he said, he was going to be burnt. *Burnt*. That was a threat he should have anticipated, considering the whole Fire Nation thing, but having just spent the morning discussing the effects of Zuko's injury, the possibilities for how deeply unpleasant this could be were kind of in the forefront of his mind.

And he was very well aware that the trap wasn’t actually for him, of course not, all he had to do was remain alive to try to force his father into taking some sort of action, it didn’t matter how much they hurt him in the meantime as long as Zhao got to play his mind games. And this was a big one, Sokka was fairly sure that burning him was going to push certain buttons for Zuko, and he just hoped he could try and avoid reacting too strongly.

Right, like that was going to happen.

Should he fight? Should he bluff and attempt to give them false information? Did it even matter? He caught Zuko's eye, he was poised in that way he had, his whole body ready to move. He would fight if Sokka gave the word. But they would lose. Sokka had to admit that he was weak from hunger and captivity and he was unskilled in hand to hand combat. It would all be on Zuko – he was a good fighter and had levels of determination that would shame most grown men, but he was much smaller than Zhao and Ro, he was unarmed, unable to bend, and half starved. They couldn't win. He shook his head slightly, hoping Zuko took the hint. Sokka was just going to have to endure whatever they did to him.

“Do you have anything to offer me?” Zhao asked.

“What do you want to know?” Sokka was proud that his voice didn’t shake too much. Spirits he was scared.

“Information on your father’s movements.”

“I don’t know anything, how can I? I haven't seen him for years. I’ve told you that. I could lie, but what’s the point?”

“What indeed. That’s very honest of you. Unfortunately, it is not the answer I want.”

Ro braced his weight on Sokka's arm, holding him still.

*Oh spirits.*

He lay a single finger on the top of Sokka’s forearm, it went the whole width of it and more. Then there was a searing, burning heat so strong it took a moment to actually feel it, but when he did he screamed, a ragged, partly surprised sound. Spirits above and below it *hurt*.

He could smell cooked meat and something in his belly recoiled from the realisation that it was coming from him. He vaguely saw movement around him, and he refocused to see Zhao backhand Zuko hard enough to send him sprawling. There was fish and rice and spilt wine all over the floor and table. He wasn’t sure if that was something he had done, or if it had been knocked over during Zhao's scuffle with Zuko.

Ro put his finger back over Sokka's arm, next to the fist wound and the pain came again. Sokka blacked out for a second, fear, agony and a bone deep exhaustion making his brain stop working for a moment.

When he came to his senses, he realised he was leaning into a warm body, seeking comfort, and he was being held almost tenderly as he sat in the chair in front of the ruined food. A hand brushed his hair out of his eyes, and he realised with horror the man holding him was Ro. He was breathing heavy and fast, he was *enjoying* this. Sokka made a small whining noise, trying to lean away from him.

Zhao had Zuko by the throat, sensibly keeping him at arm's length, although he was sporting a set of scratches to his face, as well as a bruise to his cheekbone, so he hadn’t got away from Zuko's rage completely unscathed.

As Sokka watched, Zhao slammed Zuko down on the table in front of him, he kicked wildly, a bare heel almost but not quite catching Zhao in the hip.

“He’s like a rabid fire ferret,” Ro said. He was watching avidly, one hand still holding Sokka's arm tight against the table.

Zhao grunted. “And with as much dignity,” he said, getting a hold on one of Zuko's flailing wrists and using it to flip him on to his front, sending more rice and bits of fish flying. Zuko was making a low enraged growling noise. Distantly, Sokka thought he did sound kind of like a wild animal, perhaps like a fire ferret, whatever that was.

Zhao had finally gained control in the struggle, and he slammed both Zuko's hands down beside his head, leaning his full weight flush against his captives back, trapping him against the wooden table top.

The implied threat was suddenly very clear to Sokka. He was only about two foot away from Zuko, but it felt like a mile when their eyes met. It was shocking to see real fear in his face at the situation he was in. How had this gone so wrong so quickly? Beside him, Ro made a pleased humming noise to finally see the emotion he had been after.

Zhao leaned close to Zuko's good ear. “What did I tell you about attacking me? Did I not say you would be severely punished for it?”

Sokka couldn't think of what to do. There was nothing he *could* do, just take in gulping breaths, teetering on the edge of panic. The adrenaline in his body was so great the burns on his arm had dulled to a low buzz, and instead there were just shocks of cold running under his skin. He and Zuko's gazes were still locked, he couldn't tear his eyes away. They stayed like that, time stretching strangely between them. Then something seemed to click in Zuko's head and he started to struggle under Zhao's weight, swinging his head back in an effort to strike the bigger man. Zhao chuckled at him, restraining him easily. He slid a hand under Zuko's chin, lifting his head up so he was looking at Sokka again.

“You can choose, Zuko. Take your punishment yourself, or let the little water whelp take it.”

There was another stretchy moment where Sokka was honestly unsure what was going to happen, and then Zuko snarled, shut his eyes tight and opened them with the familiar defiant anger burning yellow and bright. “I’ll take it, if you’re too much of a coward to fight me,” he rasped out, his voice only shaking a bit.

“What honour is there in fighting you? You’re nothing and no one, no nation, no name.” Zhao pushed himself upright again. “Stay down,” he instructed. Zuko did, glaring at some squished bits of fish and the scattered rice on the tabletop in front of him.

The click of Zhao's belt buckle being undone was loud and obvious in the moment of quiet stillness, and Zuko reacted like he’d been struck by lightning, immediately exploding into a flurry of movement as he tried to shove himself off the table. Zhao tutted, took hold of him by the back of his head and slammed it back on to the wood hard enough to leave him blinking and confused, a smear of blood on his forehead, matching his bloody mouth from the earlier fight. He stayed down. Sokka tried to push himself up, whether to get away or to help he wasn't actually sure, but Ro tightened his hold, keeping him in the chair and facing forward.

They were going to make him watch. The cold shivery feeling in his body was shooting down his arms in shocky bursts and he felt like he might vomit.

Zhao took off his belt and looped it double in his hand before he smirked at Sokka, like he knew *exactly* what he had been thinking was about to happen. But instead of...that, Zhao drew the belt back and struck Zuko across the shoulders with the full force of his arm. The impact was brutal and Zuko let out a pained yelp. His eyes were still a bit dazed, but he tightened his jaw and didn't make a sound at the next swing.

Or the next.

Sokka focused on the dribble of wine making its way across the table, slow and sure, like it knew where it was going. Sokka watched it and ignored the sounds of the beating. It probably didn't last very long, but it felt like an age.

Finally it was over and Zhao pulled Zuko to his feet, where he swayed a little, using one hand to brace himself against the table. Ro released Sokka and dragged him back to his cell. He had never imagined he would be so glad to be locked away again, him and Zuko both.

“I’ll be back tomorrow, Water Tribe. You better have something for me, or we will go through all this again.”

Sokka just looked at him, evil bastard. Both of them, *evil*.

On the other side of the bars Zuko thumped down next to him in something more like a controlled fall than actually sitting. He still looked completely out of it. There were bits of rice and preserved vegetables stuck to his chest and belly where he had been lying on them. Sokka eyed them for a moment. Oh well, food was food. He carefully picked the scraps off him and put them in his lap. At least the fuckers had forgotten to take back their pants. Pants were a small victory. “Zuko, you with me?”

Zuko blinked blearily at him. It wasn't clear if it was shock, the blow to the head or the beating that had knocked the sense out of him.

Sokka peered at him worriedly. “You in there, buddy? You did okay, you didn’t scream, I would have screamed.” He *had*screamed, his arm suddenly reminded him that it was burnt, *horribly* burnt. And he drew in a sharp breath. Oh that *hurt*.

“It makes him madder if I cry,” Zuko slurred. Sokka had an unpleasant feeling he wasn't talking about Zhao. But he had to pack that one away with all the rest of the fucking awful things these past weeks had taught him and deal with the *now*.

“Move round so you’re facing the other way,” Sokka said, as calm as he could. Zuko did as he was told, and Sokka got out their salve. He had nothing to wash the wounds with, so he was going to have to just do what he could and hope for the best. Some of the blows had broken skin, and the bits that weren't bloody were covered in angry red welts. Great they were going to have a matching set of scars if they ever lived long enough to heal up.

He applied a smear of the salve to his own injuries first, to take the edge off the steadily pulsing agony that was now making itself known up his forearm. Then he set about dealing with Zuko. He flinched when Sokka’s fingers touched him, tensing visibly. He had lost a lot of weight, the bumps of his spine stood out from his reddened skin and his ribs were visible. Gently rubbing the ointment into skin, Sokka wondered what *he* looked like, he hadn't had the muscle mass to lose. He probably looked like a half starved child. The thought offended him for some reason.

“Okay, I’m done,” Sokka said.

Zuko shuffled back round to face him. His eyes were clearer, more alert. “That went well,” he said thickly.

“Oh yeah, that went super well. Your control of your temper was exemplary. Lasted what? Thirty five seconds? Forty?”

“Shut up,” Zuko grumbled, but it didn't really have any bite to it, he still looked a bit shocked.

“I mean, it might be a record.”

“Shut up, Sokka, don’t make me kick you. I hurt too much for kicking.”

“Yeah, me too. Do you want a pickle?”

Zuko did some blinking at the change in topic. “Huh?”

Sokka handed him half his salvaged food. “This was all squished on you.”

Zuko took it and glared at it. “We’re eating food that was stuck to me?” He looked appalled and bitterly angry at the little bits of smooshed food in his hand. “How is this my life!” It was half whine and half shout. “This isn’t supposed to be how it is!”

Sokka could relate. “Hey now, you know what’s worse than you willingly eating pickles that have been squished onto your chest? It’s *me* willingly eating pickles that have been squished to your chest. But you know what? I’m gonna, because beggars can’t be choosers.” Sokka demonstrated by popping one of the sad looking little tubers into his mouth. It was spicy, tart and delicious, he wished there were more. “Pretty good for a chest pickle,” he said.

Zuko snorted and ate his own. He looked worn out and beat down, his lip split from Zhao's earlier backhand and the knees to his pants ripped where he had fallen. Sokka didn't want to make it worse, he really, really didn’t. But they were going to have to talk about it. About Zhao and what he was threatening – Sokka had not been imagining that, and by the look of abject fear Zuko had given him while being restrained on the table, he was aware of it too.

They had to try and plan ahead of it, Sokka didn't think he could take much more of the fear, now it was fear for Zuko too. How did the heroes in stories cope with this endless shit?

He thought about trying to broach the subject carefully, but it was hit and miss whether Zuko would pick up on his attempts at subtlety, and nuance was not his strong point. So, he would have to just get right in there. “We need to talk about what happened.”

“Why?” Zuko scrubbed at the blood on his forehead. It was sluggishly bleeding, but the cut looked small.

“So we’re both on the same page, and so we can think about how to avoid an…escalation.”

“I don’t know what there is to say.”

“We need to talk about Zhao, what he was doing, the way he is with you?”

Zuko shot him a look from under his lashes and averted his gaze. Yeah, he knew. Sokka wasn’t sure how any one could miss those rather obvious signals, although to be fair, if anyone could, it was Zuko.

“I still don’t know what there is to say about it.” Zuko said stubbornly.

“You get that he’s attracted to you right?” Sokka said, a little exasperated, why did the guy always have to make difficult things even harder?

Zuko sneered at him. “It’s not that. People aren't *attracted* to me.”

Sokka opened his mouth but nothing came out. Zuko was right, although not for the reason that he seemed to be implying. If it was as simple as that, Sokka would probably be in the same danger, but he didn’t get that feeling from Zhao at all. It was something more complicated, and it was important they untangled it so they could understand it. “So what then?” he asked.

Zuko picked at the frayed hem of his pants, fiddling with the loose threads tickling his boney ankles. “Power.”

Yeah, that made sense, that was what all this was about to Zhao; power and punishment for Zuko embarrassing him and challenging his authority. “You want to elaborate?”

Zuko didn’t look like he wanted to, although that could have also been his struggle to get his thoughts in order. “He can do whatever he wants to me, torture me, cripple me, hurt me any way he pleases, he can do that any time. You saw today, I’m too weak to fight him. But he’s not. He’s playing games. He wants to break me first.”

The thought made Sokka shiver. “What do you mean?”

Zuko sighed and picked another thread loose. “He doesn’t want to…use force, he wants me to submit.”

Submit. Yeah, that was it. The confusing forms of Zhao’s actions came together in Sokka's mind to make a solid shape. “You were aware of this?”

“My uncle warned me he might come after me, I didn’t think it would be like *this*. But that he might want to take control of me in some way.”

“What made him think that?” Sokka said cautiously. This was all ringing very true.

“He said that Zhao is loyal to the throne only insofar as he was loyal to his own ambition. He said while he works to please my father, he hates him for having the power he wants.”

Sokka kind of wished he had a chance to actually talk with Iroh, he sounded like a smart guy. Unlike his idiot nephew who had just been sitting on these insights and not sharing with the class.

Because this? This made all the sense. There was no way Zhao could or would go after Ozai, but *Zuko*, his flesh and blood, made an easier and very tempting target, and without Iroh or his title to protect him? He was fair game.

“We are going to have words about you not mentioning all this before, you dumbass,” Sokka told Zuko sternly. He had no idea if they could have done anything different, but it would have been useful to have this information *before* they walked into one of Zhao’s games.

“It’s not your problem,” Zuko snapped at him.

“It is my problem!” Sokka snapped back, his voice rising a little.

“It’s not!”

“Yes it is! Because firstly, I’m not a fucking monster, if you were anyone I would want to stop you being hurt. Secondly you aren’t *anyone*, you’re my...” *Friend.* He wasn’t sure if that was strictly speaking true, or how Zuko would take it. “...ally, we’re in this mess together, and were going to get out of it together. And thirdly, he is using me to get to you! That very much makes it my problem!”

Zuko looked mulish. “It’s something *I* have to deal with.”

“The reason that whole shit show happened was because you reacted to them hurting *me*. Did we not say don’t react? Is my pain your problem?” Sokka snorted. “No, it’s *my* problem, but you still cared about it, and still tried to stop it like a complete fucking moron!” He softened his voice a little. “We are each other's problems because we’re allies. That’s what allies do, they help each other.”

“Fine. Where do we go from here?”

Woo, a point for Sokka. Arguing with Zuko was often like beating your head against a brick wall, and every win needed to be celebrated. “We think of a new plan I guess, because that one sucked.”

Zuko thought about that for a moment. “No, I think the plan should remain the same. I pour his wine, you offer him what information you can. We know what he wants, let’s use it.”

….And now for more arguing. “No, no way. Zuko, you think you’re safe as long as you don’t give in, but you’re not. What if he gets fed up waiting? What if we get too close to port and he runs out of time for things to play out the way he wants them to?”

“Even more reason to act! If he gets close to port he’s going to have to kill me!”

“Well, he’s going to do that anyway! We should hold out in case an opportunity presents itself. What could you even have stolen off the table anyway? Chopsticks? Some fish bones? There’s no point in risking it,” Sokka said angrily.

“So you’re just going to sit and let him *burn* you?” Zuko demanded.

“Yeah, we are *both* just going to sit and let him burn me.” Sokka couldn’t believe he was actually saying this. He didn’t want to let them hurt him, spirits, two small burns on his arm were throbbing so much it made him want to cry, more were going to be unbearable. “He’s going to do it anyway, don’t give him the satisfaction of a reaction. If he gets bored, he might up the stakes, it could give us a chance. We should hold on.”

Zuko huffed angrily. “I hate this plan.”

“I do too, buddy. But...” Sokka couldn’t describe to Zuko how he’d felt in those moments he thought he was going to have to watch while Zhao forced him, semi-conscious and bleeding on the table. “It’s the best option for now,” he finished, a little pathetically.

He was going to be hurt, but there was nothing either of them could do about it, at least if they could keep Zuko in one piece long enough, he might be able to take advantage of an opportunity if one arose.

“I *hate* this,” Zuko said again.

Sokka couldn’t help but agree – but what else could they do?

………………………..

The kids were planning to escape. The likelihood of success was very slim, but the mere mention of it was the sort of thing Taiju should have reported to the admiral.

But he hadn't.

At first, he had told himself he was just waiting until he had more information, but then he had watched Zuko sitting naked in his cell and reciting a popular Fire Nation children's story in order to calm his equally naked Water Tribe cellmate. The strange sight had caused Taiju to hold his tongue.

It had been the Lady and the Volcano, a fable about a young woman cast out from her people, who had taken refuge in the caves beneath a feared volcanic mountain. She had passed her time telling stories to the spirit that dwelled there, earning its hopeless love. Taiju had told this tale to his twins when they had been small enough to sit on his knees. Hua had been particularly taken with it, shaking her little fists when the heroine had offered her own life to save the very people who had shunned her. Taiju remembered how she had gasped in wonder when the volcano spirit had spared them all out of love for the lady.

Watching Zuko tell it had hit something in him, whether it had been the resonance of the story with memories of his own children, or just the sight of Ursa’s compassion in him – there could be no other reason for the effort to calm the Water Tribe boy with his rhythmic storytelling.

So Taiju had kept his mouth shut.

Today's events had been upsetting to watch, the kids realisation of Zhao’s sick game and its inevitable conclusion had been extremely unsettling to hear put into words. It wasn't the first time Zhao had broken an enemy this way. Some of those ‘enemies’ had once been his superiors, sometimes they had been his subordinates, people who had spoken out against him.

It was disgusting behaviour, ill fitting for someone with rank, but there was nothing that could be done about it. No one who survived was stupid enough to make a fuss, as the admiral was a military favourite, and no one on this ship would ever mention what was going on, like they would never mention the fact Zuko was still alive. They were loyal – either through fear or through the knowledge their loyalty would be rewarded. And of course, there were the people who were allowed to enjoy their own distasteful things, like Ro and Chen, who was always allowed to deliver the killing blows to prisoners.

Taiju was not like them, he was a different kind of distasteful and he knew that even sick men like Ro would look down on him if they knew the truth. Spying was dirty work, contemptible and dishonourable. But someone had to do it, and his loyalty was strong enough he was willing to bear the shame of it.

The past few days as he had watched Ro waterboard a *child*, who clearly knew nothing of use, he had wondered just how much shame he could take. He was surprised that it was inaction that was causing him such turmoil, rather than having to *do* something questionable. He had not been prepared for this. Simulated drowning was always appalling to watch when done to a hardened soldier, watching a man take pleasure from doing it to a half grown kid was almost beyond Taiju’s ability to stand. It had added another layer to his morel quandary – what to do about the Water Tribe boy?

Taiju might be a spy on this ship and had no loyalty to the Admiral, but he *was* fiercely loyal to the Fire Nation, and to *his*commander who far outranked Zhao. The sensible thing to do to maintain his fealty would be for Taiju to kill Zuko, quietly and painlessly. That would be following the Fire Lords orders and prevent the torture and unpleasantness that would no doubt lead up to his inevitable death. But then what to do about the Water Tribe boy? Killing him would be acting against the Fire Nation, but leaving him to be tortured to death was not something Taiju thought he could live with. Sokka was born to an enemy, but he was just a boy. A bright, empathetic and decent *child, w*ho showed compassion and understanding towards Zuko, an enemy and one of the people who had caused him harm. He clearly hated the Fire Lord and the army, which made sense, but he seemed able to separate those things from the citizens he had met on his travels. Which was more than most men seemed able to do in wartime. It was more than Taiju had been able to do. Until now, and he hated it.

The prisoners presented him with two different problems and no easy answers. He was going to have to take Sokka’s advice and wait, perhaps orders would come through for him, perhaps the choice would be taken away from him and he could rest easy knowing he had been doing his duty.

In the meantime, maybe he could help in another way - smuggle them a little extra food, and they were going to need more bandages and salve. He would boil some water too, something to try to help prevent infection and offer a little comfort. It would have to do for now.

Feeling a little easier, but still with that endless lead weight in his belly, he was surprised to see Zhao stride into the room again. Both the boys shot to their feet at the sight of him, although Zuko staggered a little and had to use the bars to steady himself.

“What do you want?” he demanded. “Haven’t you had enough for one day?”

Sokka let out a gusty sigh and glared at his cellmate, although there was no heat in it.

Zhao looked pleased, he usually did when dealing with the prisoners, although he was still sporting some scratches on his face from his earlier struggle with Zuko. “Indeed, I just had some news and thought I would come and share it with you.”

Sokka stiffened, looking scared – no doubt making the leap to the letter Zhao had sent to his father.

Zhao ignored him, focused on Zuko as he usually was. He held up an official looking scroll. “I just had word from Captain Tsing. Three days ago General Iroh was captured in the colonies. Apparently he was looking for support in raising rebellion.” Zhao paused to smile, cruel and smug. “Seems like reports of your death may have tipped him over the edge into outright treason.”

Zuko had gone white, and even from this distance Taiju could see his hands were shaking where they were clenched by his sides.

“Unfortunately for Iroh, the Fire Lord in his wisdom, had already given orders for his immediate execution. A sentence that was carried out yesterday morning.”

“You’re lying!” Zuko yelled at an impressive volume.

“I’m not. What would be the point of lying to you? To hurt you? I can do that anyway. I can read you the missive if you want.” He unrolled the scroll. “Admiral Zhao, as per your request, I hereby inform you that the sentence of death was carried out with the sun's first light, by means of beheading, as befits a traitor to the realm...”

“You’re *lying*!” Zuko hurled himself bodily at the bars, first trying and failing to bend, his efforts barely creating a spark, then trying to reach through the bars to claw at the Admiral, who had prudently remained far enough away to avoid any such attempts. When that didn't work the boy continued to try to beat the bars out of his way, thumping and shaking them until the metal rattled alarmingly.

“Zuko! Zuko! Stop!” Sokka was desperately trying to prevent the other boy from hurting himself, but he was too far away and his friend was too distraught to pay him any mind.

“*You’re lying!”*

“Zuko. I have no reason at all to bother to lie to you, as entertaining as this temper tantrum is. The seal and letter are real.” He held it up, but Taiju doubted the boy could read it, he was in such a state.

Zhao watched Zuko as he flailed against the bars, he looked amused by the reaction and Taiju hated him for it. Who took pleasure in a child’s pain? It was true the Admiral had lost a great deal due to Zuko’s interference, and the boy had embarrassed him on a number of occasions, but in Taiju’s opinion being shamed by a teenager was an indication you should be trying to do *better*, not an invitation to take petty and cruel revenge on them when they were helpless.

Feeling powerless, Taiju watched Zhao as he enjoyed Zuko’s desperate rage and denial before he seemed to grow bored and turned to leave with a sneer. The door closed behind him with a loud clang.

“Zuko,” Sokka said, his hand still stretched through the bars. Zuko sunk down where he was and clutched at his head, fingers digging into the skin of his scalp. He let out a wordless cry of grief that struck Taiju right to the bones.

Taiju had no idea if news of Iroh’s death was real or not, but Zuko's distress at the loss of his uncle was horrible to watch no matter the truth, it was genuine and heartbreaking. It just solidified in Taiju’s mind the reality that had been creeping up on him, the thing that made this harder; whatever had happened between Zuko and his father was not due to the prince being heartless like Ro or Chen, he *had* a conscience and he had compassion. Taiju didn’t even think whatever had caused his banishment had been outright ambition. Nothing he had said has implied that. Even his treasonous crime of freeing the Avatar seemed to be a misguided attempt to earn back his fathers affection. It was clear his intention was not to betray the Fire Nation, he was just a lonely, miserable kid.

It ripped at something in Taiju’s heart, and he felt it right down to the core of himself.

After ten minutes or so, Zuko pulled himself awkwardly and painfully to his feet, glaring down at the water tribe boy who was sitting on the other side of the bars and watching him solemnly. “Get up.”

“What?”

“Get up.”

Sokka got up, tatty red trousers hanging off his skinny frame.

Zuko pointed at him. “Stand with your feet shoulder width apart, your body's weight traveling down your spine and through your heels into the ground.”

“Okay. Why?”

“You can’t fight, I’ll teach you.”

Sokka looked offended. “I can fight!”

“I kicked the crap out of you three times in as many minutes the first time we met.”

“Oy, I got you with my boomerang.”

“Do you *have* a boomerang?”

“No, good point, well made. Okay, I’m standing. But you really shouldn't be, don’t go moving around after all the work I put into patching you up.”

“You smeared gunk on me, it’s hardly work. I want you to focus on your breathing. Feel the energy in your body and the ground through your heels.” There was something strong and determined in the boy’s face that made Taiju shiver to watch. It didn’t seem to matter how many times something knocked him over, he always got right back up again.

Sokka got into a passable position, and Zuko began to show him a few slow, basic movements.

“This can be the basis for both hand to hand and fighting with a bladed weapon,” Zuko said.

“Cool!” The kid’s eyes lit up like any teenager being offered the opportunity to learn how to hit something with a sword. “Okay, what do I do?”

“Just practice this, every day, every time we can, then I’ll show you the next ones.” The flowing slow motions of Zuko’s arms, and his steady steps seemed to have calmed him a little, but his eyes were still burning with anger. “I don’t believe what he said. I *don’t* believe it, I won’t until I see proof.”

Sokka nodded, wobbling through a step. “They lied about your execution, no reason to believe they’re telling the truth about your uncle.”

Zuko made a sharp, angry gesture, only wincing a little as it pulled the wounds on his back. “I refuse to sit here and do nothing.”

“Okay!” Sokka bowed to him, deep and a little awkward. Bowing didn’t seem to be a Water Tribe thing. “Teach me, Sifu Zuko!”

“You can start by actually doing the stance I showed you.”

“Yeah, yeah, I’m doing it. Weight down through the heels, got it.”

Taiju watched them practice, Zukos’s movements flowing, despite his injuries, and Sokka not as graceful, but showing a great deal of promise in the speed he picked up the simple forms.

What was he supposed to do with this? What was he supposed to *do*? If he committed treason, his family would be forfeit, his wife could be punished, his children at best would lose any hope of a decent position, at worst they could be *executed.* How could he risk that?

He would wait and hope. What else could he do?

## Chapter 9

### Notes

#### Chapter Summary

The universe didn't seem to like Zuko much, but it also seemed reluctant to actually try to take the final step to snuff him out. Probably too scared he would crawl out of his grave and yell at it.

#### Chapter Notes

Warnings for this chapter: Normal blanket warnings and tags apply, plus: Wound care for burns and brief references to debridement, humiliation, nonsexual nudity, brief discussion of canon typical racism.

Many thanks to my beta burkesl17, who has managed to wrangle my dyslexic nonsense. All mistakes are my own.

### Text

So, it turned out burns were more painful and more trouble than being flogged or beaten.

Luckily, Zuko seemed to know what he was doing as he carefully cleaned and washed Sokka’s wounds. Removing the dead and useless skin was excruciating and deeply unpleasant, but apparently vital for preventing infection. Sokka didn’t watch, just held his arms through the bars and thought about murder while he tried not to whimper in pain and revulsion.

They had fallen into an ugly routine. In the morning, Ro would burn Sokka while Zhao would offer relief if they agreed to his terms, which remained more or less the same. Sokka had to give him information he didn’t have, and Zuko would need to attend dinner in his quarters – which definitely seemed like an escalation to Sokka.

Zuko was clearly struggling with having to witness the burning, but had so far held out from doing anything stupid in response. It appeared that Zhao's attempts to break him with the news of Iroh's death had in fact done the opposite, it seemed to have filled him with double the determination – he *would* get out, and he *would* find his uncle. He refused to discuss it, other than to insist Zhao had been lying and that he would find Iroh alive. Sokka had no desire to argue with this, whatever got him through the day was a good thing in Sokka’s book.

Then, after the burning, when Zhao and Ro left, Taiju would bring them fresh bandages and clean, boiled water, as well as restocking their salve. He had even smuggled them a little dried fruit on one occasion. He looked like he was having a bad week, dark shadows had formed under his eyes and his jaw was always clenched. Sokka thought it was probably a sign the man had a conscience lurking somewhere inside, but whenever he attempted to engage him in conversation, he would remain stoic and silent, like he couldn't even hear Sokka’s voice. He wasn’t sure what to make of it.

Later, after the wounds had been treated and bound, in an effort to escape the pain and to distract them both from the looming presence of tomorrow, Sokka practiced the slow flowing forms Zuko was teaching him. Learning to fight with his hands would be very useful, but it was the possibility of learning the sword that kept him on his feet and moving. One day, he would stab Ro in his sick, stupid face with a sword. He thought about it a lot, whenever he was trying and failing to sleep through the pain now radiating from both his forearms. It was hard to escape from the constant hurt, and the knowledge they were going to scar badly if he survived long enough for them to heal properly.

He tried not to think about what it must have been like for Zuko, this kind of pain on his *face*, knowing he would be disfigured, and having to have the dead tissue scrubbed away. Sokka hoped Iroh or whoever had done that for him had had a gentler hand than Zuko did – he really was crap at moderating his strength. Sokka thought it was partly because he was so enraged at the injuries it made him want to scrub them from existence.

It was only a matter of time before things got worse though. After today's burning, while Sokka shuddered and tried not to scream, Ro had wrapped a hand around his ankle, heating his palm and fingers until just shy of burning. Sokka had stared at him, stupefied and exhausted. He couldn’t believe this was happening to him, the world had taken on a bizarre sense of unreality with each new horror still somehow a surprise. Ro had looked at Sokka and smiled before releasing him, unscalded, and returning him to his cell.

After they had gone, Zuko looked at him with an intense expression, solemn and angry. “If they burn your joints, they might cripple you,” he said bluntly.

And who knew Sokka still had more fear in him? Apparently he did.

“When you’re burnt deep enough to scar, the skin pulls tight,” Zuko elaborated, as he reached through the bars and demonstrated by pinching the skin of Sokka's ankle hard between his fingers.

“Ow!” Sokka kicked at him with his other foot. Why did everything have to hurt all the time?

Zuko withdrew his hand, but he still looked very serious. “When that happens on a joint, it restricts movement.”

Oh, sort of like how Zuko couldn't fully open his left eye. Well, that was horrifying and terrifying. “So that’s a thing people do? The Fire Nation is the worst,” Sokka said miserably, trying to summon some anger to help drive out the new anxiety.

“Actually it’s a thing that some Earth Kingdom troops started to do to captured firebenders, it’s a serious punishment. It takes a lot of heat to burn a firebender, and it’s hard to bend properly if you don’t have full range of motion,” Zuko continued with a shrug. “It can be done of course, people can still bend without a hand or foot, but you have to relearn things. They see it as some sort of poetic justice I guess.”

“That’s kind of sick.” Sokka felt that weird disappointment he always got when he heard about other nations doing shitty things, wasn't it enough that the Fire Nation was awful? There was no need to compete with them for the title of the worst bunch of arseholes ever.

Zuko shrugged again. “People do bad things in war.”

“Understatement of the century.”

And speaking of bad things, what would happen if Sokka couldn’t move properly? There would be no hope of escape at all, except for death, which was looking more and more likely to be the only way out of this endless nightmare. “How do I stop them?” he blurted, he could hear the fear in his voice and felt embarrassed for it, but he couldn't pull it back. He didn’t want to die, even with how things were, he wanted to *live*.

“We can’t. Nothing has changed in the last week, they’re going to step up their game and make it worse to force us to do what they want.” Zuko's jaw firmed and his brow furrowed, clear indicators that he was going gearing up for an argument. “We have to go back to the plan.”

He was right, but after the way the last attempt had gone, Sokka was very reluctant to do any such thing. “It’s so risky,” he said quietly, ashamed to even be considering it.

“We don’t have any other options, Sokka.”

“Let’s say we go for it, what are we hoping to gain?” Sokka asked, attempting to inject a little bit of rational thought into his conflicted feelings.

“We need something to pick the lock. We need to know where we are, and what direction we need to go when we escape.”

What direction, yeah that was going to be an issue. Zuko's plan seemed to extend to the point where they flung themselves into the sea, and then it was just a case of hoping they weren’t a hundred miles from shore. “So before we do actual escaping, we need to find out our heading and current location. How do we even do that?”

Zuko tipped his head back and frowned at the ceiling. “I guess the only way to know where we are is for someone to tell us. Then if one of us can get on deck to see the stars, we could use that to confirm direction. I’ve been traveling at sea for years, I’m familiar enough with the charts and maps I might be able to figure it out with just a bit of information.”

“So what you’re saying is we need to get someone to talk to us, and we need to get on deck. That’s not likely to happen.” But even as he said it, Sokka’s thoughts went to Taiju and his shadowed eyes. Maybe he was the key, if he could just give them a tiny bit of information, maybe… Of course there was another problem, they had to get out of the cells – but what if that happened out of order? What if they achieved lock picking before navigation? “If we get out, get to the sea, and don’t know where we are, or find out we’re miles from shore, what then? Go back to our cells with an apology?”

Zuko huffed. “We’ll burn that bridge when we get to it.”

Sokka hid his smile, examining his fingernails. “Pretty sure that is not how that saying goes, but it kind of tracks for you, so we’ll roll with it.”

“Whatever. To be honest, I would prefer to take my chances in the ocean than let these bastards kill me.”

Sokka made an unhappy face, looking at his bandaged arms. “I’m kind of inclined to agree with you there. But I would really rather not die at all. Alright, our objectives remain to secure some sort of lock picking object, and find out where we are. Or get a look at the stars, as unlikely as that is.”

“Agreed.”

“How?”

Zuko scoffed. “You know how.”

“If you’re suggesting we should attempt to get Zhao to take you to his rooms, I’m going to kick you.”

“We *have* to. You think I want to? Trust me I don’t, but there is no other option.”

He was right. But when it all went wrong, Sokka wasn’t sure he was going to be able to live with it. “Okay, when we do this, you’re going to have to fake giving up. That’s not going to be easy,” he told Zuko, reluctantly. He also had a sneaky suspicion that acting was not going to be Zuko's strong suit.

“I can do it,” Zuko said, with perhaps more determination than confidence.

“Please stop doing this,” Zuko said to Zhao later, as wooden as an old gnarled tree stump.

“Oh, are you asking for favours now?”

“Yes. We are ready to discuss terms,” Zuko said, he seemed to be trying to keep his voice sounding cowed. It was not altogether successful.

Zhao seemed amused. Zuko's attempts to look humbled mostly just made him look like he had eaten something bad and was about to start puking. But Sokka was still quite impressed with him despite the dubious delivery.

Zhao must have known they were up to something, but seemed unable to resist the temptation of letting it play out. “So you’re prepared to make a deal?”

“Yes, I’ll serve your wine, your dinner, whatever. Just please stop, for a few days at least.”

“If I have you serve dinner, you’ll have to clean yourselves up,” Zhao said, eyeing them both like they were something nasty the cat had dragged in.

Oh boy, last time he had said that, things had not gone well, and Sokka was attached to his pants, he didn’t want to lose them again.

“Provide us clean clothes and water to wash and we will.” Zuko insisted, echoing the same sentiment he had the previous time Zhao had decided to make them clean up.

Zhao stepped close to the bars. “The thing is, Zuko, I don’t trust you to behave. So here’s the deal: I will take you to serve me, but we will bring the little water rat too. If you misbehave, even once, I will burn out an eye, do you understand me?”

Oh.

They could have the pants, Sokka was far, far more attached to his eyes. He didn’t know what to be more frightened of, the fact this was even a threat they had on their list of fun and evil things to do to prisoners, or the fact that his sight remaining intact was reliant on Zuko holding his temper and his tongue.

This was a terrible, terrible, awful idea. It had been the first time, and it remained so the second time.

“Okay,” Zuko said. His voice was a little shaky in a way that Sokka didn’t think he was faking. “Then you’ll stop?”

“No. If you have behaved yourself, then we can discuss terms for stopping.”

“That’s not fair!” Zuko’s volume climbed with his frustration.

Zhao raised an eyebrow and Zuko snapped his mouth shut.

Sokka wished he could retract his eyes back into his head like some kind of bug. He *wished* he could do that. Zuko had lasted about fifteen seconds before raising his voice. Oh *spirits*.

“I’ll send water and a tub down and you can bathe and change into clean clothes. I don’t want you smelling like this in my quarters.”

It sounded like heaven, but Sokka was a little distracted by the likelihood he would be returning to the cell minus an *eye*. He knew Zuko would do his best, but he also knew from past experience, that might not be quite enough.

Zhao really did send down a whole tub. Two soldiers, directed by Chen, carried it into Zuko's cell, and then Sokka was ushered in as well. A different cell! What an exciting change of scene.

“Wash,” Chen told them. He looked at them with nothing but contempt. Sokka felt the same in turn, although the sight of him no longer elicited the same crushing terror as it had during the beginning of his stay on the murder boat, Sokka still hated the man, he was a sick bastard like the rest. One day, he would get a sword to the gut too.

After the soldiers left them alone, Sokka stood for a moment, unsure. This was the first time they had been in the same cell and they looked at each other for a moment, eyes a little wide.

Sokka wasn’t sure what started him moving, and he only registered he *had* when he slung his arms around Zuko in a tight, desperate hug. He was all boney and stiff, but the feeling of holding a person like this was the best thing Sokka had felt in weeks. Although it would be better if Zuko wasn't just standing there in rigid surprise, with his arms sticking out awkwardly at his sides. Sokka decided to ignore it and just keep hugging. Slowly, Zuko's arms came up to hold him back and after a moment of getting used to the idea they suddenly tightened around him into an almost crushing embrace. Even skinny and starving the guy was shockingly strong. Sokka dropped his head into the crook of his neck and just breathed in the slightly stinky smell of him, relishing the warmth of his skin and the feel of his heart beating rapidly where their bodies touched. He didn’t want to leave this comfort, and he was embarrassed to find himself on the edge of tears.

“We should bathe,” Zuko said thickly, close to his ear. “Before the water gets cold.” He sounded as affected as Sokka felt, and knowing him, probably had no idea how to process those feelings. So Sokka gave him a manly thump on the back and stepped away. He didn't trust himself to speak, so he just nodded.

The tub was a good size, with high sides and there was a warming scent coming from the water, perhaps from some sort of fragrant oil, clearly Zhao had not been kidding about them not stinking up the cabin. They had also been provided with fresh clothes, two pails of water, a bar of soap and cloth for washing and drying.

Zuko shucked his pants with no hesitation and went straight for one of the smaller pails. He crouched down and scooped out a little water to taste. He made a face. “Sea water.” He sighed heavily, clearly he had been hoping they would be able to drink some of it. Resigned, he started to use it to wash himself down.

That seemed like a good idea actually, as they were going to have to share the bathwater, and whoever went last would have to sit in the other person's dirt. *Gross*. Sokka eyed the amount of grime coming off Zuko as he washed and decided to fight to the death to go in the bath first. He did get a little lost in cleaning himself, as he was just as disgustingly dirty as Zuko, and washing the filth off felt amazing.

“Get in,” Zuko said when they were done. “Don’t get your arms wet.”

Score! Sokka was very pleased he was going first, very pleased, especially as Zuko could probably squish him like a bug in a fight. “Anyone ever tell you you’re very bossy?” he asked anyway, just to be annoying.

“I’m not bossy, people are just idiots and don’t do what they’re supposed to.”

“Uh huh.”

Sokka climbed into the tub awkwardly, the water was still warm and felt like bliss. The smell alone was going to his head, it was so *nice*. It felt like an unthinkable luxury.

“Don’t get your arms in the water,” Zuko said again, as Sokka settled.

“Just give me a minute.” Sokka closed his eyes. “How am I supposed to wash without my arms?” he muttered, feeling muscles he hadn’t even realised he had, slowly relaxing. This was amazing.

His eyes shot open again as Zuko put a hand on his back and shoved him unceremoniously up the bath, his butt bouncing along the bottom uncomfortably. “Oy!”

The water sloshed as Zuko got into the tub behind him, his annoyingly long legs sliding flush along Sokka's own, half on top of him. He squeaked in a way that could possibly be considered unmanly. *Okay then*. Zuko had some really strange ideas about what was proper and not proper, and Sokka was never sure what was Zuko-ness and what was Fire Nation-ness.

Whatever weird code of conduct he had going on in his head, Sokka seemed to have been placed in the category where nudity was not an issue, and now apparently communal bathing was chill too. Okay, fine, he could roll with it, although it was a little outside Sokka's comfort zone. Having said that, most of his life was currently outside his comfort zone, so whatever.

“At some point, we are going to have to have a conversation about boundaries,” he said, letting himself relax again.

“You can’t wash, so I’ll have to help,” Zuko said, sounding irritated. He slapped what Sokka assumed was a soapy cloth onto his back.

Reasonable, of course Zuko could probably help from *outside* the tub, but whatever logic his brain followed was not the same of Sokka's, so maybe it hadn’t occurred to him, or maybe there was some weird Fire Nation meaning to it Sokka was missing, or possibly he was just cold and had wanted to sit in the bath. Who knew?

Sokka decided all awkwardness was forgiven as the cloth started moving across his skin in brisk movements, carefully cleaning and rinsing his back, neck and upper arms. It was *wonderful.* Not exactly gentle, because Zuko, but to feel a touch that was not causing him pain, was such a relief. How had he not realised how much he craved and needed this before it had been gone?

“Tip your head back a bit.”

Sokka did and Zuko poured water from his hands over his hair, carefully avoiding his face, which was thoughtful of him, as Sokka didn’t think he could handle that after the drowning thing. His nightmares had been taken over by the feel of it, and even now it made him shudder.

Zuko flopped the soapy cloth on Sokka's head and started scrubbing like he was trying to obliterate his scalp. “Hey, gently!” Sokka yelped.“A little less force and you can still get it clean, I promise.”

“Your hair looks stupid like this,” Zuko told him sternly. “It’s too long on top and hangs funny.”
